\mathcal{I}_{*}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\Delta_{0}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Applying the first part of Theorem M3, we obtain

\breve{\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(3.25)

Then, we can apply the second part of Theorem M0 to deduce

\osc\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\quad\underline{\mathcal{O}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(3.26)

Note that (
3.26
) implies
\mathcal{I}_{0}\leq\epsilon_{0}
where
\mathcal{I}_{0}
is defined in Theorem M3. Thus, we may apply the second part of Theorem M3 to obtain

\mathcal{O}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(3.27)

Notice that (
3.24
), (
3.26
) and (
3.27
) implies that we can apply Theorem M4 to extend
\mathcal{K}
to
\wideparen{\mathcal{K}}:=\wideparen{V}(u_{0},{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu)
for a
\nu
sufficiently small. We denote
\wideparen{\mathcal{O}}
and
\wideparen{\mathcal{R}}
the norms associated to the new foliation
(\wideparen{u},{\underline{u}})
. We have

\wideparen{\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\wideparen{\mathcal{R}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\wideparen{\osc}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

as a consequence of Theorem M4. We deduce that
\wideparen{V}(u_{0},{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu)
with the double null foliation
(\wideparen{u},{\underline{u}})
satisfies all the properties in Definition
3.8
, and so
\aleph({\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu)\neq\emptyset
, which is a contradiction. Thus, we have
{\underline{u}}_{*}=+\infty
, which implies property 1 of Theorem
3.1
. Moreover, we have

\mathcal{O}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\mathcal{R}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

(3.28)

in the whole exterior region, which implies property 2 of Theorem
3.1
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

4
Curvature estimates (Theorem M1)

In this section, we prove Theorem M1 by the
r^{p}
–weighted estimate method introduced in
[
7
]
and applied to Bianchi equations in
[
11
]
and
[
18
]
. For convenience, we recall the statement below.

Theorem M1
.

Assume that

\mathcal{O}_{(0)}\leq\epsilon_{0},\qquad\mathcal{O}\leq\epsilon,\qquad\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}\leq\epsilon,\qquad\osc\leq\epsilon.

(4.1)

Then, we have

\mathcal{R}\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}.

(4.2)

Figure 2:
Domain of integration
V(u,{\underline{u}})

Lemma 4.1
.

We have the following estimates:

|\Gamma_{g}|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{r^{2}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},\qquad\quad|\Gamma_{b}|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{r|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}},\qquad\quad|\Gamma_{a}|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{r^{2}},

(4.3)

and

|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{p,S}\lesssim\epsilon,\qquad|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}|_{p,S}\lesssim\epsilon,\qquad p\in[2,4].

(4.4)

Proof.

It follows directly from the assumption
\mathcal{O}\leq\epsilon
, Definition
2.14
and the Sobolev inequality of Proposition
2.29
.
∎

4.1
Estimates for general Bianchi pairs

The following lemma provides the general structure of Bianchi pairs. It will be used repeatedly in this section. See Lemma 8.24 in
[
18
]
for an analog version under the assumption of axial polarization.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
k=1,2
and
a_{(1)}
,
a_{(2)}
real numbers. Then, we have the following properties.

1.

If
\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}\in\mathfrak{s}_{k}
and
\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}\in\mathfrak{s}_{k-1}
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{3}(\psi_{(1)})+a_{(1)}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\psi_{(1)}&=-kd_{k}^{*}(\psi_{(2)})+h_{(1)},\\
\nabla_{4}(\psi_{(2)})+a_{(2)}\tr\chi\,\psi_{(2)}&=d_{k}(\psi_{(1)})+h_{(2)}.\end{split}

(4.5)

Then, the pair
(\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)})
satisfies for any real number
p

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}e_{3})+k\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}e_{4})\\
+&\left(2a_{(1)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\underline{\chi}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+k\left(2a_{(2)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\chi|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
=&2kr^{p}\sdiv(\psi_{(1)}\cdot\psi_{(2)})+2r^{p}\psi_{(1)}\cdot h_{(1)}+2kr^{p}\psi_{(2)}\cdot h_{(2)}-2r^{p}\underline{\omega}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\\
-&2kr^{p}\omega|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+pr^{p-1}\left(e_{3}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right)|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+kpr^{p-1}\left(e_{4}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi\right)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}.\end{split}

(4.6)

2.

If
\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}\in\mathfrak{s}_{k-1}
and
\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}\in\mathfrak{s}_{k}
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{3}(\psi_{(1)})+a_{(1)}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\psi_{(1)}&=d_{k}(\psi_{(2)})+h_{(1)},\\
\nabla_{4}(\psi_{(2)})+a_{(2)}\tr\chi\,\psi_{(2)}&=-kd_{k}^{*}(\psi_{(1)})+h_{(2)}.\end{split}

(4.7)

Then, the pair
(\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)})
satisfies for any real number
p

\displaystyle\begin{split}&k\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}e_{3})+\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}e_{4})\\
+&k\left(2a_{(1)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\underline{\chi}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\left(2a_{(2)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\chi|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
=&2r^{p}\sdiv(\psi_{(1)}\cdot\psi_{(2)})+2kr^{p}\psi_{(1)}\cdot h_{(1)}+2r^{p}\psi_{(2)}\cdot h_{(2)}-2kr^{p}\underline{\omega}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\\
-&2r^{p}\omega|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+kpr^{p-1}\left(e_{3}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right)|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+pr^{p-1}\left(e_{4}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi\right)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}.\end{split}

(4.8)

Remark 4.3
.

Note that the Bianchi equations can be written as systems of equations of the type (
4.5
) and (
4.7
). In particular

•

the Bianchi pair
(\alpha,\beta)
satisfies (
4.5
) with
k=2
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{1}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=2
,

•

the Bianchi pair
(\beta,(\widecheck{\rho},-\sigma))
satisfies (
4.5
) with
k=1
,
a_{(1)}=1
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{3}{2}
,

•

the Bianchi pair
((\widecheck{\rho},\sigma),\underline{\beta})
satisfies (
4.7
) with
k=1
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{3}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=1
,

•

the Bianchi pair
(\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha})
satisfies (
4.7
) with
k=2
,
a_{(1)}=2
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{1}{2}
.

Proof.

By a direct computation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{Div}e_{4}=\mathbf{D}_{\gamma}e_{{4}}^{\gamma}=-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{D}_{4}e_{4},e_{3})-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{D}_{3}e_{4},e_{4})+\mathbf{g}^{AB}\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{D}_{A}e_{4},e_{B})=-2\omega+\tr\chi,

and similarly

\displaystyle\mathbf{Div}e_{3}=-2\underline{\omega}+\tr\underline{\chi}.

For
\psi_{(1)}
and
\psi_{(2)}
satisfying (
4.5
), we compute

\displaystyle\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}e_{3})+k\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}e_{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2})+\mathbf{Div}(e_{3})r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+k\nabla_{4}(r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2})+k\mathbf{Div}(e_{4})r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle pr^{p-1}e_{3}(r)|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+2r^{p}\psi_{(1)}\cdot\nabla_{3}\psi_{(1)}+r^{p}(\tr\underline{\chi}-2\underline{\omega})|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle kpr^{p-1}e_{4}(r)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+2kr^{p}\psi_{(2)}\cdot\nabla_{4}\psi_{(2)}+kr^{p}(\tr\chi-2\omega)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle pr^{p-1}e_{3}(r)|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+2r^{p}\psi_{(1)}\cdot\left(-a_{(1)}\tr\underline{\chi}\psi_{(1)}-kd_{k}^{*}\psi_{(2)}+h_{(1)}\right)+r^{p}\tr\underline{\chi}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}-2r^{p}\underline{\omega}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle kpr^{p-1}e_{4}(r)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+2kr^{p}\psi_{(2)}\cdot\left(-a_{(2)}\tr\chi\psi_{(2)}+d_{k}\psi_{(1)}+h_{(2)}\right)+kr^{p}\tr\chi|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}-2kr^{p}\omega|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle pr^{p-1}\left(e_{3}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right)|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+2r^{p}\psi_{(1)}\cdot\left(-kd_{k}^{*}\psi_{(2)}+h_{(1)}\right)+\left(1-2a_{(1)}+\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\underline{\chi}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle kpr^{p-1}\left(e_{4}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi\right)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+2kr^{p}\psi_{(2)}\cdot\left(d_{k}\psi_{(1)}+h_{(2)}\right)+\left(1-2a_{(2)}+\frac{p}{2}\right)kr^{p}\tr\chi|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2r^{p}\underline{\omega}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}-2kr^{p}\omega|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2k\sdiv(\psi_{(1)}\cdot\psi_{(2)})+pr^{p-1}\left(e_{3}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right)|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+kpr^{p-1}\left(e_{4}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi\right)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2r^{p}\psi_{(1)}\cdot h_{(1)}+2kr^{p}\psi_{(2)}\cdot h_{(2)}+\left(1-2a_{(1)}+\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\underline{\chi}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\left(1-2a_{(2)}+\frac{p}{2}\right)kr^{p}\tr\chi|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2kr^{p}\omega|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}-2r^{p}\underline{\omega}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}.

Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}e_{3})+k\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}e_{4})+\left(2a_{(1)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\underline{\chi}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\left(2a_{(2)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)kr^{p}\tr\chi|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle=2kr^{p}\sdiv(\psi_{(1)}\cdot\psi_{(2)})+2r^{p}\psi_{(1)}\cdot h_{(1)}+2kr^{p}\psi_{(2)}\cdot h_{(2)}-2r^{p}\underline{\omega}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}-2kr^{p}\omega|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle+pr^{p-1}\left(e_{3}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right)|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+kpr^{p-1}\left(e_{4}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi\right)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2},

which implies (
4.6
). The proof of (
4.8
) is similar and left to the reader. This concludes the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Proposition 4.4
.

We denote

V:=V(u,{\underline{u}}),\qquad C_{u}^{V}:=C_{u}\cap V,\qquad{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}:={\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}\cap V.

(4.9)

For
\psi_{(1)}
,
\psi_{(2)}
and
h_{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}
satisfying (
4.5
) or (
4.7
), we have the following properties for
q=0,1
and all
(u,{\underline{u}})\in\mathcal{K}
.

•

In the case of
2+p-4a_{(1)}>0
and
4a_{(2)}-2-p>0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+r^{p-1}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}||h_{(1)}^{(q)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}||h_{(2)}^{(q)}|.\end{split}

(4.10)

•

In the case of
2+p-4a_{(1)}>0
and
4a_{(2)}-2-p=0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}||h_{(1)}^{(q)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}||h_{(2)}^{(q)}|.\end{split}

(4.11)

•

In the case of
2+p-4a_{(1)}\leq 0
and
4a_{(2)}-2-p\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}||h_{(1)}^{(q)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}||h_{(2)}^{(q)}|.\end{split}

(4.12)

•

In the case of
2+p-4a_{(1)}>0
and
4a_{(2)}-2-p\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p-1}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}||h_{(1)}^{(q)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}||h_{(2)}^{(q)}|.\end{split}

(4.13)

Remark 4.5
.

In the sequel, For a sum of terms of the form
\Gamma\cdot R^{(1)}
, we ignore the terms have same or even better decay. For example, we write

\displaystyle\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\alpha^{(1)}=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)},

since
\alpha^{(1)}
decays better than
\beta^{(1)}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\omega,\,\underline{\omega}\in\Gamma_{a}
. Applying Lemma
2.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle e_{3}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}=\frac{\overline{\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}}}{2\Omega}r-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}=\frac{r}{2\Omega}(\overline{\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}}-\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})\in r\Gamma_{g},

and similarly

\displaystyle e_{4}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi\in r\Gamma_{g}.

Applying (
4.3
), we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{V}r^{p}|\Gamma_{a}|\left(|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\right)\\
&\lesssim\epsilon\int_{V}r^{p-2}\left(|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\right)\\
&\lesssim\epsilon\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(|u|^{-2}\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\right)du+\epsilon\int_{|u_{0}|}^{\underline{u}}\left(|{\underline{u}}|^{-2}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\right)d{\underline{u}}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon\sup_{u}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\right)\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}|u|^{-2}du+\epsilon\sup_{{\underline{u}}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\right)\int_{|u_{0}|}^{{\underline{u}}}|{\underline{u}}|^{-2}d{\underline{u}}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon\sup_{u}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\right)+\epsilon\sup_{{\underline{u}}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\right).\end{split}

(4.14)

Integrating (
4.6
) or (
4.8
), reminding that
\tr\chi-\frac{2}{r}\in\Gamma_{a}
,
\tr\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{r}\in\Gamma_{a}
, and that
\Gamma_{g}
decays better than
\Gamma_{a}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+\int_{V}(2+p-4a_{(1)})r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)r^{p-1}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}||h_{(1)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}||h_{(2)}|+r^{p}|\Gamma_{a}||\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\Gamma_{a}||\psi_{(2)}|^{2}.

Taking the supremum of
u
and
{\underline{u}}
and applying (
4.14
), we obtain for
\epsilon
small enough

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{u}\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\sup_{\underline{u}}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
+&\int_{V}(2+p-4a_{(1)})r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)r^{p-1}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}||h_{(1)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}||h_{(2)}|,\end{split}

(4.15)

which implies (
4.10
)–(
4.13
) hold in correspond cases when
q=0
.

Next, we consider the case
q=1
. Assume that
(\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)})
satisfies (
4.5
).
8
8

8

The case of
(\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)})
satisfies (
4.7
) is similar.
We multiply (
4.5
) by
\Omega
and differentiate it by
\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}
to obtain

\displaystyle[\not{{\mathfrak{d}}},\Omega\nabla_{3}]\psi_{(1)}+\Omega\nabla_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(1)})+a_{(1)}r\nabla(\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})\cdot\psi_{(1)}+a_{(1)}\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}\,\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-k(r\nabla\Omega)d_{k}^{*}(\psi_{(2)})-k\Omega d_{k}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(2)})+(r\nabla\Omega)h_{(1)}+\Omega\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}h_{(1)},

\displaystyle[\not{{\mathfrak{d}}},\Omega\nabla_{4}]\psi_{(2)}+\Omega\nabla_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(2)})+a_{(2)}r\nabla(\Omega\tr\chi)\cdot\psi_{(2)}+a_{(2)}\Omega\tr\chi\,\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(r\nabla\Omega)d_{k}(\psi_{(1)})+\Omega d_{k}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(1)})+(r\nabla\Omega)\cdot h_{(2)}+\Omega\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}h_{(2)}.

Applying Proposition
2.24
, we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(1)})+a_{(1)}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(1)}&=-kd_{k}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(2)})+h_{(1)}^{(1)}+(\Gamma_{b}\cdot\psi_{(1)})^{(1)}+(\Gamma_{g}\cdot\psi_{(2)})^{(1)},\\
\nabla_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(2)})+a_{(2)}\tr\chi\,\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(2)}&=d_{k}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(1)})+h_{(2)}^{(1)}+\left(\Gamma_{g}\cdot(\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)})\right)^{(1)}.\end{split}

(4.16)

Integrating (
4.16
), and proceeding similarly as in (
4.14
), we deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{u}\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{p}|\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\sup_{\underline{u}}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{p}|\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
+&\int_{V}(2+p-4a_{(1)})r^{p-1}|\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)r^{p-1}|\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(1)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}^{(1)}||h_{(1)}^{(1)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}^{(1)}||h_{(2)}^{(1)}|.\end{split}

(4.17)

Combining (
4.15
) and (
4.17
), this concludes the proof of Proposition
4.4
.
∎

The following lemma allows us to obtain
|u|
–decay of curvature along
\Sigma_{0}\cap V(u,{\underline{u}}_{*})
.

Lemma 4.6
.

We have the following estimate for
p\leq s
:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V(u,{\underline{u}})}r^{p}\left(|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}+|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}+|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}}{|u|^{s-p}}.

Proof.

For fixed
u
and
{\underline{u}}
, we have from Lemma
3.4

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V(u,{\underline{u}})}r^{p}\left(|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}+|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}+|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{2|u^{\prime}|\geq|u|\}}{r^{\prime}}^{p}\left(|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}+|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}+|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{2|u^{\prime}|\geq|u|\}}|u^{\prime}|^{p-s}{r^{\prime}}^{s}\left(|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}+|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}+|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|u|^{p-s}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{2|u^{\prime}|\geq|u|\}}{r^{\prime}}^{s}\left(|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}+|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}+|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}}{|u|^{s-p}}.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
4.6
.
∎

4.2
Estimates for the Bianchi pair
(\alpha,\beta)

Proposition 4.7
.

We have the following estimate:

\mathcal{R}_{0}[\alpha]^{2}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}[\beta]^{2}+\mathcal{R}_{1}[\alpha]^{2}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}[\beta]^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

(4.18)

Proof.

We recall the following equations of Corollary
2.20
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{4}\beta+2\tr\chi\beta&=d_{2}\alpha+h[\beta_{4}],\\
\nabla_{3}\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\alpha&=-2d_{2}^{*}\beta+h[\alpha_{3}],\end{split}

(4.19)

which correspond to
\psi_{(1)}=\alpha
,
\psi_{(2)}=\beta
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{1}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=2
,
h_{(1)}=h[\alpha_{3}]
,
h_{(2)}=h[\beta_{4}]
and
k=2
in (
4.5
). Taking
p=s
and recalling that
s\in[4,6]
, we have

2+p-4a_{(1)}=s>0,\qquad 4a_{(2)}-2-p=6-s\geq 0.

(4.20)

We apply (
4.10
) to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s}|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{s}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{s-1}|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{s}|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}+r^{s}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{s}|\alpha^{(1)}||h[\alpha_{3}]^{(1)}|+r^{s}|\beta^{(1)}||h[\beta_{4}]^{(1)}|.

It remains to estimate
|\alpha^{(1)}||h[\alpha_{3}]^{(1)}|
and
|\beta^{(1)}||h[\beta_{4}]^{(1)}|
. Recalling from Corollary
2.20
that
9
9

9

We used the fact that
\sigma
,
\beta
decay better than
\rho
.

\displaystyle h[\alpha_{3}]=4\underline{\omega}\alpha+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\rho,\qquad h[\beta_{4}]=(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{g})\cdot\beta,

(4.21)

we have
10
10

10

We ignore the terms which decay better.

\displaystyle|\alpha^{(1)}||h[\alpha_{3}]^{(1)}|+|\beta^{(1)}||h[\beta_{4}]^{(1)}|\lesssim|\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}||\alpha^{(1)}||\alpha|+|\Gamma_{a}||\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+|\Gamma_{g}||\alpha^{(1)}||\rho^{(1)}|+|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\alpha||\rho|.

Applying (
4.3
) to estimate
\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{V}r^{s}|\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}||\alpha^{(1)}||\alpha|&\lesssim\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{s}|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{s}|\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}|^{2}|\alpha|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{R}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}r^{s}|\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}|^{2}|\alpha|^{2}\right)d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{R}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{-3}|u|^{1-s}|r^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}|^{2}_{4,S}|r^{\frac{s+2}{2}}\alpha|^{2}_{4,S}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{-3}|u|^{1-s}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}|u|^{-1}|u|^{\frac{1-s}{2}}du\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.22)

Applying (
4.3
) to estimate
\Gamma_{a}
, we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{V}r^{s}|\Gamma_{a}||\beta^{(1)}|^{2}&\lesssim\epsilon\int_{V}r^{s-2}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}\lesssim\epsilon\int_{|u_{0}|}^{\underline{u}}|{\underline{u}}|^{-2}d{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{s}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}\right)\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.23)

Applying (
4.3
) to estimate
\Gamma_{g}
, we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{V}r^{s}|\Gamma_{g}||\rho^{(1)}||\alpha^{(1)}|&\lesssim\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{\epsilon}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{s}|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{s-4}|\rho^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{s-8}d{\underline{u}}\int_{S}|r^{2}\rho^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{s-8}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\frac{1}{|u|^{\frac{7-s}{2}}}du\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.24)

We also have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{V}r^{s}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\rho||\alpha|&\lesssim\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{s}|\alpha|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{s}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\rho|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{R}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{S(u,{\underline{u}})}r^{s}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\rho|^{2}\right)d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{R}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{s-8}|u|^{3-s}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}_{4,S}|r^{\frac{5}{2}}\rho|^{2}_{4,S}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{s-8}|u|^{3-s}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}du\\
&\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}|u|^{\frac{s-7}{2}}|u|^{\frac{3-s}{2}}du\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.25)

Thus, we obtain

\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s}|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{s}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{s-1}|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

(4.26)

This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.7
.
∎

4.3
Estimates for the Bianchi pair
(\beta,(\widecheck{\rho},-\sigma))

Proposition 4.8
.

We have the following estimate:

\mathcal{R}_{0}[\beta]^{2}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}[(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)]^{2}+\mathcal{R}_{1}[\beta]^{2}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}[(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)]^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

(4.27)

Proof.

We recall the following Bianchi equations

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\widecheck{\rho}+\frac{3}{2}\tr\chi\widecheck{\rho}

\displaystyle=\sdiv\beta+h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4}],

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\sigma+\frac{3}{2}\tr\chi\sigma

\displaystyle=-\sdiv{{}^{*}\beta}+h[\sigma_{4}],

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\beta+\tr\underline{\chi}\beta

\displaystyle=\nabla\widecheck{\rho}+{{}^{*}\nabla}\sigma+h[\beta_{3}],

which can be written in the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{4}(\widecheck{\rho},-\sigma)+\frac{3}{2}\tr\chi(\widecheck{\rho},-\sigma)&=d_{1}\beta+h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4},\sigma_{4}],\\
\nabla_{3}\beta+\tr\underline{\chi}\beta&=-d_{1}^{*}(\widecheck{\rho},-\sigma)+h[\beta_{3}],\end{split}

(4.28)

where
h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4},\sigma_{4}]=(h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4}],h[\sigma_{4}])
. Applying (
4.11
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\beta
,
\psi_{(2)}=(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)
,
a_{(1)}=1
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{3}{2}
,
h_{(1)}=h[\beta_{3}]
,
h_{(2)}=h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4},\sigma_{4}]
and
p=4
, we obtain from Lemma
4.6

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{4}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}\left(r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+r^{4}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}\right)\\
&+\int_{V}r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}\cdot h[\beta_{3}]^{(1)}|+\int_{V}r^{4}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})\cdot h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4},\sigma_{4}]^{(1)}|\\
\lesssim&|u|^{4-s}\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\int_{V}r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}\cdot h[\beta_{3}]^{(1)}|+\int_{V}r^{4}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})\cdot h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4},\sigma_{4}]^{(1)}|.\end{split}

(4.29)

Recall from Corollary
2.20
that

h[\beta_{3}]=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\beta},\qquad h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4},\sigma_{4}]=\Gamma_{b}\cdot\alpha+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\widecheck{\rho}.

Hence, we have

h[\beta_{3}]^{(1)}=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\beta}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\underline{\beta},\qquad h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4},\sigma_{4}]^{(1)}=\Gamma_{b}\cdot\alpha^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}\cdot\alpha+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\widecheck{\rho}.

Hence, we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{V}r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}||h[\beta_{3}]^{(1)}|+r^{4}|(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)^{(1)}||h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4},\sigma_{4}]^{(1)}|\\
\lesssim&\int_{V}r^{4}|\Gamma_{a}||\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+r^{4}|\Gamma_{g}||\beta^{(1)}||\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|+r^{4}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta||\underline{\beta}|.\end{split}

(4.30)

For the first term in (
4.30
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{4}|\Gamma_{a}||\beta^{(1)}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}|u|^{-2}du\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}\right)\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{4-s}.

For the second term in (
4.30
), we infer

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{4}|\Gamma_{g}||\beta^{(1)}||\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{1}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}du\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{du}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\frac{\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{\frac{s-4}{2}}}\frac{\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{\frac{s}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s-4}}.

For the third term in (
4.30
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{4}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta||\underline{\beta}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{V}r^{4}\frac{\epsilon}{|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}}|\beta|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{4}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\underline{\beta}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{du}{|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}}\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{4}|\beta|^{2}+\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{4}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\underline{\beta}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{du}{|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}}\frac{\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s-4}}+\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}r^{-2}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}_{4,S}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}\underline{\beta}|^{2}_{4,S}du

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}|u|^{s-4}}+\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}r^{-2}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{2s-4}}du

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s-4}}+\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{3}{2}s-\frac{5}{2}}}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{d{\underline{u}}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s-4}}.

Injecting all the estimates above into (
4.30
), we deduce

\int_{V}r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}||h[\beta_{3}]^{(1)}|+r^{4}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})||h[\widecheck{\rho}_{4},\sigma_{4}]^{(1)}|\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{4-s}.

Combining with (
4.29
), we obtain (
4.27
). This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.8
.
∎

4.4
Estimates for the Bianchi pair
((\widecheck{\rho},\sigma),\underline{\beta})

Proposition 4.9
.

We have the following estimates:

\mathcal{R}_{0}[(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)]^{2}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}[\underline{\beta}]^{2}+\mathcal{R}_{1}[(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)]^{2}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}[\underline{\beta}]^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

(4.31)

Proof.

We recall the following Bianchi equations:

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\underline{\beta}+\tr\chi\,\underline{\beta}

\displaystyle=d_{1}^{*}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)+h[\underline{\beta}_{4}],

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)+\frac{3}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)

\displaystyle=-d_{1}\underline{\beta}+h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3},\sigma_{3}],

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3},\sigma_{3}]:=(h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3}],h[\sigma_{3}])=\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\alpha},\qquad h[\underline{\beta}_{4}]=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\underline{\beta}.\end{split}

(4.32)

We apply (
4.12
) with
\psi_{(1)}=(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)
,
\psi_{(2)}=\underline{\beta}
,
a_{(1)}=1
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{3}{2}
,
h_{(1)}=h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3},\sigma_{3}]
,
h_{(2)}=h[\underline{\beta}_{4}]
and
p=2
, we obtain from Lemma
4.6
and Proposition
4.8
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{2}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}+\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}\left(r^{2}(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}+r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)+\int_{V}r|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}\\
&+\int_{V}r^{2}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})\cdot h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3},\sigma_{3}]^{(1)}|+r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}\cdot h[\underline{\beta}_{4}]^{(1)}|\\
\lesssim&|u|^{2-s}\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\int_{|u|}^{{\underline{u}}}|{\underline{u}}|^{-3}d{\underline{u}}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{4}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})|^{2}\\
&+\int_{V}r^{2}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})\cdot h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3},\sigma_{3}]^{(1)}|+r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}\cdot h[\underline{\beta}_{4}]^{(1)}|\\
\lesssim&|u|^{2-s}\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})\int_{|u|}^{{\underline{u}}}|u|^{4-s}|{\underline{u}}|^{-3}d{\underline{u}}\\
&+\int_{V}r^{2}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})\cdot h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3},\sigma_{3}]^{(1)}|+r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}\cdot h[\underline{\beta}_{4}]^{(1)}|\\
\lesssim&|u|^{2-s}(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})+\int_{V}r^{2}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})\cdot h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3},\sigma_{3}]^{(1)}|+r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}\cdot h[\underline{\beta}_{4}]^{(1)}|.\end{split}

(4.33)

Applying (
4.32
) we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{V}r^{2}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})\cdot h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3},\sigma_{3}]^{(1)}|+r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}\cdot h[\underline{\beta}_{4}]^{(1)}|\\
\lesssim&\int_{V}r^{2}|\Gamma_{g}||\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}||\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)}|+r^{2}|\Gamma_{a}||\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\underline{\beta}|^{2}.\end{split}

(4.34)

For the first term in (
4.34
), we infer

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{2}|\Gamma_{g}||\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}||\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}||\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{d{\underline{u}}}{|{\underline{u}}|^{2}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{4}|\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{d{\underline{u}}}{|{\underline{u}}|^{2}}\frac{\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{\frac{s}{2}}}\frac{\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{\frac{s-4}{2}}}\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{2-s}.

For the second term in (
4.34
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{2}|\Gamma_{a}||\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}\lesssim\epsilon\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}|{\underline{u}}|^{-2}d{\underline{u}}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}\ r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{2-s}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}|{\underline{u}}|^{-2}d{\underline{u}}\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{2-s}.

For the third term in (
4.34
), we infer

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{2}|r\nabla\Gamma_{g}||\underline{\beta}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{2}|\underline{\beta}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{2}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\underline{\beta}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\frac{\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{\frac{s-2}{2}}}\left(\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{du}{|{\underline{u}}|^{4}}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}_{4,S}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}\underline{\beta}|^{2}_{4,S}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{R}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\frac{1}{|u|^{\frac{s-2}{2}}}\left(\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{du}{|{\underline{u}}|^{4}}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{s-3}}\frac{\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\frac{1}{|u|^{\frac{s-2}{2}}}\frac{1}{|{\underline{u}}|^{2}}\frac{1}{|u|^{s-\frac{5}{2}}}\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{2-s}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{2}|(\widecheck{\rho}^{(1)},\sigma^{(1)})\cdot h[\widecheck{\rho}_{3},\sigma_{3}]^{(1)}|+r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}\cdot h[\underline{\beta}_{4}]^{(1)}|\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{2-s}.

Combining with (
4.33
), we obtain (
4.31
). This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.9
.
∎

4.5
Estimates for the Bianchi pair
(\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha})

Proposition 4.10
.

We have the following estimates:

\mathcal{R}_{0}[\underline{\beta}]^{2}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}[\underline{\alpha}]^{2}+\mathcal{R}_{1}[\underline{\beta}]^{2}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}[\underline{\alpha}]^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

(4.35)

Proof.

We recall the following Bianchi equations:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{4}\underline{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\,\underline{\alpha}&=-\nabla\widehat{\otimes}\underline{\beta}+h[\underline{\alpha}_{4}],\\
\nabla_{3}\underline{\beta}+2\tr\underline{\chi}\,\underline{\beta}&=-\sdiv\underline{\alpha}+h[\underline{\beta}_{3}],\end{split}

(4.36)

where

\displaystyle h[\underline{\alpha}_{4}]=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\underline{\alpha},\qquad h[\underline{\beta}_{3}]=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\underline{\beta}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\alpha}.

(4.37)

Applying (
4.12
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\underline{\beta}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\underline{\alpha}
,
a_{(1)}=2
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{1}{2}
,
h_{(1)}=h[\underline{\beta}_{3}]
,
h_{(2)}=h[\underline{\alpha}_{4}]
and
p=0
, we obtain from Lemma
4.6

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{-1}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\beta}_{3}]^{(1)}|+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\alpha}_{4}]^{(1)}|\\
\lesssim&|u|^{-s}\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\int_{|u_{0}|}^{\underline{u}}r^{-3}d{\underline{u}}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}r^{2}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\beta}_{3}]^{(1)}|+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\alpha}_{4}]^{(1)}|\\
\lesssim&|u|^{-s}\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})\int_{|u_{0}|}^{\underline{u}}r^{-3}d{\underline{u}}|u|^{2-s}+\int_{V}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\beta}_{3}]^{(1)}|+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\alpha}_{4}]^{(1)}|\\
\lesssim&|u|^{-s}(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})+\int_{V}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\beta}_{3}]^{(1)}|+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\alpha}_{4}]^{(1)}|.\end{split}

(4.38)

Applying (
4.37
), we infer
11
11

11

Recall that the
\Gamma_{a}
before
\underline{\alpha}
only contains
\omega
.

\displaystyle\int_{V}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\beta}_{3}]^{(1)}|+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\alpha}_{4}]^{(1)}|\lesssim\int_{V}|\Gamma_{a}||\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\underline{\alpha}|^{2}.

(4.39)

For the first term in (
4.39
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{V}|\Gamma_{a}||\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\lesssim\epsilon\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}|{\underline{u}}|^{-2}d{\underline{u}}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}|\underline{\alpha}|^{2}\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{-s}.

For the second term in (
4.39
), we infer

\displaystyle\int_{V}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\underline{\alpha}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}|\underline{\alpha}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\underline{\alpha}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{R}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}|u|^{-\frac{s}{2}}\left(\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{du}{|{\underline{u}}|^{4}}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{4,S}^{2}|r^{\frac{1}{2}}\underline{\alpha}|_{4,S}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}|{\underline{u}}|^{-2}|u|^{-\frac{s}{2}}\left(\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{du}{|u|^{2s-2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}|u|^{-\frac{s}{2}}|{\underline{u}}|^{-2}|u|^{\frac{3-2s}{2}}\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{-s}.

Thus, we obtain

\int_{V}|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\beta}_{3}]^{(1)}|+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}||h[\underline{\alpha}_{4}]^{(1)}|\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}|u|^{-s}.

Combining with (
4.38
), we obtain (
4.35
). This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.10
.
∎

4.6
Estimate for
\nabla_{4}\alpha

Proposition 4.11
.

We have the following estimates:

\mathcal{R}_{1}[\alpha_{4}]^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

(4.40)

Proof.

We recall from (
2.32
)

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\mathring{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-2d_{2}^{*}\not{\alpha}+\frac{4\alpha}{r}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\beta,

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\not{\alpha}+\frac{5}{2}\tr\chi\,\not{\alpha}

\displaystyle=d_{2}\mathring{\alpha}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\beta.

Applying (
4.10
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\mathring{\alpha}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{\alpha}
,
a_{(1)}=0
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{5}{2}
,
h_{(1)}=\frac{4\alpha}{r}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\beta
,
h_{(2)}=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\beta
,
k=2
and
p=s
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{C_{u}}r^{s}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}}r^{s}{|\not{\alpha}|^{2}}+\int_{V}r^{s-1}\big(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2}\big)\\
\lesssim&\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\int_{V}r^{s-1}|\mathring{\alpha}||\alpha|+r^{s}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{a}||\beta^{(1)}|+r^{s}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta|.\end{split}

(4.41)

First, we have for all
\delta>0

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{s-1}|\mathring{\alpha}||\alpha|\leq\delta\int_{V}r^{s-1}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4\delta}\int_{V}r^{s-1}|\alpha|^{2}.

Combining with (
4.26
), we deduce from (
4.41
), for
\delta
small enough

\displaystyle\int_{C_{u}}r^{s}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}}r^{s}{|\not{\alpha}|^{2}}+\int_{V}r^{s-1}\big(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}+\int_{V}r^{s}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{a}||\beta^{(1)}|+r^{s}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta|.

We have

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{s}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{a}||\beta^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{V}r^{s-2}|\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha}||\beta^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\frac{1}{|u|^{\frac{8-s}{2}}}\left(\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s}|\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{8-s}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-4}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

We also have

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{s}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{V}r^{s}|\mathring{\alpha}||\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta|+\int_{V}r^{s}|\not{\alpha}||\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\left(\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s}|\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\beta|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{R}\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\,r^{s}\int_{S}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\beta|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{R}\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\,r^{s-10}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}_{4,S}|r^{\frac{7}{2}}\beta|_{4,S}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{s-10}|u|^{8-2s}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\,|u|^{\frac{s-9}{2}}|u|^{4-s}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{s}|\not{\alpha}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{s-1}\left(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2}\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

Recalling that

\displaystyle\mathring{\alpha}=r^{-4}\nabla_{4}(r^{5}\alpha)=r\nabla_{4}\alpha+5e_{4}(r)\alpha,

we deduce from Proposition
4.7

\displaystyle\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s+2}|\nabla_{4}\alpha|^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.11
.
∎

4.7
Estimate for
\nabla_{3}\underline{\alpha}

Proposition 4.12
.

We have the following estimates:

\mathcal{R}_{1}[\underline{\alpha}_{3}]^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{R}(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

(4.42)

Proof.

We recall from (
2.33
)

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=-2d_{2}^{*}\underline{\not{\alpha}}+\frac{4\underline{\alpha}}{r}+(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}\cdot\underline{\alpha},

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\underline{\not{\alpha}}+\frac{5}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\underline{\not{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=d_{2}\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}+(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}+\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}\cdot\underline{\alpha}.

Applying (
4.12
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\underline{\not{\alpha}}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{5}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=0
,
h_{(1)}=\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}\cdot\underline{\alpha}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}+(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}=\frac{4\underline{\alpha}}{r}+\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}\cdot\underline{\alpha}+(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}
,
k=2
and
p=-2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{-2}|\underline{\not{\alpha}}|^{2}+\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{-2}|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{-2}(|\underline{\not{\alpha}}|^{2}+|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|^{2})+\int_{V}r^{-3}|\underline{\not{\alpha}}|^{2}+r^{-3}|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}||\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|+r^{-2}|\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}||\underline{\alpha}|(|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|+|\underline{\not{\alpha}}|).

First, we have from Lemma
4.6

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{-2}(|\underline{\not{\alpha}}|^{2}+|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|^{2})\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}}{|u|^{s+2}}.

Notice that we have from Proposition
4.10

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{-3}|\underline{\not{\alpha}}|^{2}\lesssim\int_{|u|}^{{\underline{u}}}r^{-3}d{\underline{u}}\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}|\underline{\not{\alpha}}|^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s+2}},

and

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{-3}|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}||\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{-2}\left(\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{-2}|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d{\underline{u}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{-2}\left(\frac{\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s+2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}d{\underline{u}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{\mathcal{R}(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{2}|u|^{s+1}}d{\underline{u}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{R}(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|^{s+2}}.

We also have

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{-2}|\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}||\underline{\alpha}|(|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|+|\underline{\not{\alpha}}|)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{-2}d{\underline{u}}\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}(|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|)|\underline{\alpha}||\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}|\underline{\alpha}|^{2}|\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{\frac{s}{2}}}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{-3}d{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}du|r^{\frac{1}{2}}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}_{4,S}|r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}|^{2}_{4,S}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{\frac{s}{2}}}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{-3}d{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}du\frac{\epsilon^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{2s}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{3s+3}{2}}}.

Combining the above estimates, we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{-2}|\underline{\not{\alpha}}|^{2}+\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{-2}|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{R}(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s+2}}.

Combining with Proposition
4.10
, we infer

\displaystyle\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}|\nabla_{3}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}\lesssim\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{-10}|\nabla_{3}(r^{5}\underline{\alpha})|^{2}+r^{-2}|\underline{\alpha}|^{2}\lesssim\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{-2}|\mathring{\underline{\alpha}}|^{2}+r^{-2}|\underline{\alpha}|^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{R}(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}}{|u|^{s+2}}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.12
.
∎

4.8
Estimate for
\overline{\rho}

Proposition 4.13
.

We have the following estimate for
p\in[2,4]
:

|r^{3}\overline{\rho}|_{\infty,S}(u,{\underline{u}})\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}.

(4.43)

Proof.

We recall the Bianchi equation

\nabla_{3}\overline{\rho}+\frac{3}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\overline{\rho}=h[\overline{\rho}_{3}].

Applying Lemma
2.30
, we infer for
p\in[2,4]

\displaystyle|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\overline{\rho}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}h[\overline{\rho}_{3}]|_{p,S}du

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}\cdot(\underline{\alpha},\overline{\rho})|_{p,S}du

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\frac{\mathcal{R}}{|u|^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon\mathcal{R}}{r^{2}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\right)du

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}.

Since
\overline{\rho}
is constant on
S(u,{\underline{u}})
, this concludes the proof of Proposition
4.13
.
∎

4.9
End of the proof of Theorem M1

Proposition 4.14
.

We have the following estimate:

\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}\lesssim\mathcal{R}_{0}+\mathcal{R}_{1}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}.

(4.44)

Proof.

It is sufficient to prove that

\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}\lesssim\mathcal{R}_{0}+\mathcal{R}_{1},\qquad\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}\lesssim\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1},

which is a direct consequence of Propositions
2.20
and
2.28
.
∎

Finally, we deduce from Proposition
4.7
-
4.14
that

\mathcal{R}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{R}(\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{R}^{2}.

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain for
\epsilon
small enough,

\mathcal{R}\lesssim\mathfrak{R}_{0},

this concludes the proof of Theorem M1.

5
Ricci coefficients estimates (Theorem M3)

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem M3, which we recall below for convenience.

Theorem M3
.

Assume that

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\leq\epsilon,\qquad\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}\leq\Delta_{0},\qquad\mathcal{O}^{*}({\underline{C}}_{*})\leq\mathcal{I}_{*},\qquad\breve{\underline{\mathcal{O}}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)\leq\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0},

(5.1)

then, we have

\breve{\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(5.2)

If we assume in addition that

\underline{\mathcal{O}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)\leq\mathcal{I}_{0},

(5.3)

then, we have

\mathcal{O}\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(5.4)

In this section, we always assume
p\in[2,4]
.

5.1
Estimates for
\mathcal{O}_{0}(\Omega\omega)
and
\mathcal{O}_{0}(\Omega\underline{\omega})

Proposition 5.1
.

We have the following estimates:

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\omega|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}\\
|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\underline{\omega}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.\end{split}

(5.5)

Proof.

By the null structure equations (
2.29
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{3}(\Omega\omega)=\widehat{F}+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\rho,\qquad\mathbf{D}_{4}(\Omega\underline{\omega})=\widehat{\underline{F}}+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\rho,

(5.6)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{F}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\zeta\cdot(\eta+\underline{\eta})-\frac{1}{2}\Omega(\underline{\eta}\cdot\eta-2\zeta^{2})=\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g},

\displaystyle\widehat{\underline{F}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\Omega\zeta\cdot(\eta+\underline{\eta})-\frac{1}{2}\Omega(\underline{\eta}\cdot\eta-2\zeta^{2})=\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}.

Apply Lemma
2.30
, we obtain

\displaystyle|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\underline{\omega}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\underline{\omega}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}}_{*})+\int_{{\underline{u}}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\widehat{\underline{F}}|_{p,S}+\int_{{\underline{u}}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\rho|_{p,S},

\displaystyle|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\omega|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\omega|_{p,S}(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\widehat{F}|_{p,S}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\rho|_{p,S}.

Notice that we have

|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}(\widehat{F},\widehat{}\underline{F})|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{4}|u|^{s-3}},\qquad|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\rho|_{p,S}\lesssim r^{-3}|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\rho|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\Delta_{0}}{r^{3}}.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\underline{\omega}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\underline{\omega}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}}_{*})+\epsilon^{2}+\Delta_{0}\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},

\displaystyle|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\omega|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\omega|_{p,S}(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})+\epsilon^{2}+\Delta_{0}\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

which concludes the proof.
∎

5.2
Estimates for
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widecheck{\Omega\omega})
and
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widecheck{\Omega\underline{\omega}})

Proposition 5.2
.

We have the following estimates:

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{2+\frac{s-3}{6}-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{3}}(r\nabla)^{q}\widecheck{\Omega\omega}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},\qquad q=0,1,\\
|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\widecheck{\Omega\underline{\omega}}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},\qquad q=0,1.\end{split}

(5.7)

Proof.

First, we derive an evolution equation for
\nabla(\Omega\omega)
. Applying (
2.29
) and Corollary
2.22
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Omega\nabla_{3}\nabla(\Omega\omega)

\displaystyle=[\Omega\nabla_{3},\nabla](\Omega\omega)+\nabla(\Omega\nabla_{3}(\Omega\omega))

\displaystyle=-\Omega\underline{\chi}\cdot\nabla(\Omega\omega)+\nabla(\Omega\nabla_{3}(\Omega\omega))

\displaystyle=-\Omega\underline{\chi}\cdot\nabla(\Omega\omega)+\nabla\left(\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{2}\rho+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}\nabla(\Omega\omega)+\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{2}\nabla\rho+(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{g},

which implies

\nabla_{3}\nabla(\Omega\omega)+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\nabla(\Omega\omega)=\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\Omega\rho)+r^{-1}(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

(5.8)

Since we cannot control
\nabla\rho
in
L^{p}(S)
, we use a renormalization method. We define
\omega^{\dagger}
by

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\omega^{\dagger}=\frac{1}{2}\Omega\sigma\quad\mbox{in }\mathcal{K},\qquad\omega^{\dagger}=0\quad\mbox{on }S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}}).

(5.9)

Applying Corollary
2.22
, we have

\displaystyle\Omega\nabla_{3}{{}^{*}\nabla\omega^{\dagger}}={{}^{*}\nabla}(\Omega\nabla_{3}\omega^{\dagger})+[\Omega\nabla_{3},{{}^{*}\nabla}]\omega^{\dagger}=\frac{1}{2}{{}^{*}\nabla(\Omega^{2}\sigma)}-\Omega\underline{\chi}\cdot{{}^{*}\nabla\omega^{\dagger}},

which implies

\nabla_{3}{{}^{*}\nabla\omega^{\dagger}}+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}({{}^{*}\nabla\omega^{\dagger}})=\frac{1}{2}{{}^{*}\nabla}(\Omega\sigma)+\Gamma_{b}\cdot(\sigma,{{}^{*}\nabla}\omega^{\dagger}).

(5.10)

We recall the following Bianchi equation from Proposition
2.18
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\beta+\tr\underline{\chi}\beta

\displaystyle=\nabla\rho+{{}^{*}\nabla}\sigma+2\underline{\omega}\beta+2\widehat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\beta}+3(\eta\rho+{{}^{*}\eta}\sigma)

\displaystyle=\nabla\rho+{{}^{*}\nabla}\sigma+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\beta},

which implies
12
12

12

Notice that
\beta\nabla_{3}\Omega=-2\Omega\underline{\omega}\beta=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta
and
(\rho,\sigma)\nabla\Omega=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{g}
decay better than the R.H.S. of (
5.11
).

\nabla_{3}(\Omega\beta)+\tr\underline{\chi}(\Omega\beta)=\nabla(\Omega\rho)+{{}^{*}\nabla}(\Omega\sigma)+r^{-1}\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.

(5.11)

We introduce a new quantity:

{\varkappa}:=\nabla(\Omega\omega)+{{}^{*}\nabla\omega^{\dagger}}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\beta.

(5.12)

Thus, we have the following equation:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{3}{\varkappa}&=\nabla_{3}\nabla(\Omega\omega)+\nabla_{3}{{}^{*}\nabla}\omega^{\dagger}-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{3}(\Omega\beta)\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\nabla(\Omega\omega)-\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}({{}^{*}\nabla}\omega^{\dagger})+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}\,\beta+r^{-1}(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot{{}^{*}\nabla}\omega^{\dagger}\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\varkappa+\frac{1}{4}\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}\,\beta+r^{-1}(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot{{}^{*}\nabla}\omega^{\dagger}.\end{split}

(5.13)

Denoting
\langle\omega\rangle:=(-\Omega\omega,\omega^{\dagger})
, then
13
13

13

Recall that
d_{1}^{*}(f,g)=-\nabla f+{{}^{*}\nabla}g.

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{1}^{*}\langle\omega\rangle&={\varkappa}+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\beta.\end{split}

(5.14)

Applying Proposition
2.13
to (
5.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla(\Omega\omega)|_{p,S}+|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\omega^{\dagger}|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}{\varkappa}|_{p,S}+|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\beta|_{p,S}.

(5.15)

Also, applying Lemma
2.30
to (
5.13
), we have

\displaystyle\;\;\;\,\,\,|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}{\varkappa}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}{\varkappa}|_{p,S}(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\beta|_{p,S}+|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{p,S}+|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{b}\cdot{{}^{*}\nabla}\omega^{\dagger}|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\Delta_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{\Delta_{0}}{r^{\frac{7}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-4}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{4}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{3}|u|^{s-2}}+\frac{\epsilon}{r|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}}|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}({{}^{*}\nabla}\omega^{\dagger})|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r^{2+\frac{s-3}{6}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{3}}}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{\epsilon}{r|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}}|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}{\varkappa}|_{p,S},

where at the last step we used for all
s>3

\displaystyle 2+\frac{s-3}{6}\leq\min\left\{3,\frac{s+1}{2}\right\}.

(5.16)

Applying Gronwall inequality, we infer

\displaystyle|r^{3+\frac{s-3}{6}-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{3}}{\varkappa}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

Injecting it into (
5.15
) and recall that

\displaystyle|r^{3+\frac{s-3}{6}-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{3}}\beta|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{\frac{7}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-4}{2}}\beta|_{p,S}\lesssim\Delta_{0},

we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{3+\frac{s-3}{6}-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{3}}\nabla(\Omega\omega)|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.\end{split}

(5.17)

Applying Poincaré inequality, we conclude the first estimate of (
5.7
). The second estimate is similar and left to the reader. This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.2
.
∎

5.3
Estimates for
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widecheck{\Omega})
and
r\left|\Omega-\frac{1}{2}\right|

Proposition 5.3
.

We have the following estimates:

\displaystyle\left|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\widecheck{\Omega}\right|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},\qquad q=0,1,

(5.18)

\displaystyle\sup r\left|\Omega-\frac{1}{2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(5.19)

Proof.

We recall that

\Omega\nabla_{4}\Omega=\Omega^{2}\nabla_{4}\log\Omega=-2\Omega^{2}\omega.

Differentiating by
r\nabla
and applying Proposition
2.24
, we deduce

\displaystyle\Omega\nabla_{4}(r\nabla\Omega)

\displaystyle=[\Omega\nabla_{4},r\nabla]\Omega-2\Omega r\nabla(\Omega\omega)-2r\nabla\Omega(\Omega\omega)

\displaystyle=O(r\nabla(\Omega\omega))+r\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}.

Applying Lemma
2.30
and Proposition
5.2
, we infer

\displaystyle|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\Omega|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\Omega|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}}_{*})+\int_{{\underline{u}}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}\left(|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla({\Omega\omega})|_{p,S}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{3}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},

where we used the fact that
\Omega=\frac{1}{2}
on
{\underline{C}}_{*}
. Combining with Poincaré inequality, we deduce (
5.18
). On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\Omega\nabla_{4}\left(\Omega-\frac{1}{2}\right)=-2\Omega^{2}\omega.

Recall that Propositions
5.1
,
5.2
and
2.29
implies

\displaystyle|\Omega\omega|_{\infty,S}\lesssim\frac{\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r^{2}}.

Applying Lemma
2.30
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\Omega-\frac{1}{2}\right|\lesssim\int_{{\underline{u}}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}|\Omega\omega|_{\infty,S}\lesssim\frac{\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r},

which implies (
5.19
). This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.3
.
∎

5.4
Estimate for
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widecheck{\Omega\tr\chi})

Proposition 5.4
.

We have the following estimate:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\widecheck{\Omega\tr\chi}\right|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},\qquad q=0,1.\end{split}

(5.20)

Proof.

We recall the following equation from Proposition
2.17
:

\Omega\nabla_{4}(\Omega\tr\chi)+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\tr\chi(\Omega\tr\chi)=-4\Omega\tr\chi(\Omega\omega)-\Omega^{2}|\widehat{\chi}|^{2}.

(5.21)

We derive an evolution equation for
r\nabla(\Omega\tr\chi)
. Differentiating (
5.21
) by
r\nabla
and applying Proposition
2.24
to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Omega\nabla_{4}(r\nabla(\Omega\tr\chi))+\Omega\tr\chi(r\nabla\Omega\tr\chi)\\
=&[\Omega\nabla_{4},r\nabla](\Omega\tr\chi)-4r\nabla(\Omega\tr\chi)\Omega\omega-4\Omega\tr\chi\,r\nabla(\Omega\omega)+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\\
=&\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+O(\nabla(\Omega\omega)).\end{split}

(5.22)

Applying Lemma
2.30
and Proposition
5.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla(\Omega\tr\chi)|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla(\Omega\tr\chi)|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}}_{*})+\int_{{\underline{u}}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla(\Omega\omega)|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\Gamma_{a}|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{*}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\int_{\underline{u}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}\frac{\Delta_{0}+\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r^{1+\frac{s-3}{6}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{3}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

Applying Poincaré inequality, we obtain (
5.20
). This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.4
.
∎

5.5
Estimate for
\left|r^{2}\left(\overline{\Omega\tr\chi}-\frac{1}{r}\right)\right|

Proposition 5.5
.

We have the following estimate:

\displaystyle\sup\left|r^{2}\left(\overline{\Omega\tr\chi}-\frac{1}{r}\right)\right|\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(5.23)

Proof.

Applying Lemma
2.4
, we obtain

\frac{d}{d{\underline{u}}}\frac{1}{r}=-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\frac{\partial r}{\partial{\underline{u}}}=-\frac{1}{2r}\overline{\Omega\tr\chi}=-\frac{1}{2r}\Omega\tr\chi+\frac{\widecheck{\Omega\tr\chi}}{2r}.

(5.24)

Recalling (
5.21
) and denoting

W:=\overline{\Omega\tr\chi}-\frac{1}{r},

(5.25)

we deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{d}{d{\underline{u}}}W+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\tr\chi W&=\frac{1}{2}W(\widecheck{\Omega\tr\chi})+\frac{1}{2}\overline{(\widecheck{\Omega\tr\chi})^{2}}-\overline{4\Omega\tr\chi(\Omega\omega)+\Omega^{2}|\widehat{\chi}|^{2}}\\
&=-4\overline{\Omega\tr\chi\Omega\omega}+W\cdot\Gamma_{g}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}.\end{split}

(5.26)

Applying Proposition
5.1
and
W\in\Gamma_{a}
, we infer

\displaystyle|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\tr\chi\,\Omega\omega|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r^{2}},

\displaystyle|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}W\cdot\Gamma_{g}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})+|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r^{3}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

Using Lemma
2.30
, we have

\displaystyle|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}W|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r}.

Recalling that
\nabla W=0
, applying Proposition
2.29
, we deduce

\displaystyle|r^{2}W|_{\infty,S}\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

In view of (
5.25
), this concludes the proof of Proposition
5.5
.
∎

5.6
Estimate for
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widecheck{\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}})

Proposition 5.6
.

We have the following estimate:

\left|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\widecheck{\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}}\right|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},\qquad q=0,1.

(5.27)

Proof.

We recall the following equation from Proposition
2.17
:

\Omega\nabla_{3}(\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}(\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})=-4\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}(\Omega\underline{\omega})-\Omega^{2}|\widehat{\underline{\chi}}|^{2}.

(5.28)

As in (
5.22
), we derive an evolution equation for
r\nabla(\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})
. Differentiating (
5.28
) by
r\nabla
and applying Proposition
2.24
to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Omega\nabla_{3}(r\nabla(\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}))+\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}(r\nabla\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})\\
=&[\Omega\nabla_{3},r\nabla](\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})-4r\nabla(\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})\Omega\underline{\omega}-4\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}\,r\nabla(\Omega\underline{\omega})+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}\\
=&\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}+O(\nabla(\Omega\underline{\omega})).\end{split}

(5.29)

Applying Lemma
2.30
, we obtain

\displaystyle|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla{(\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla{(\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})}|_{p,S}(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla{(\Omega\underline{\omega})}|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}|+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{p,S}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla{(\Omega^{-1}\tr\underline{\chi})}|_{p,S}(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\Omega|_{p,S}

\displaystyle+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\left(|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla{(\Omega\underline{\omega})}|_{p,S}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{2}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{s-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},

where we used Propositions
5.2
,
5.3
and the fact that
\breve{\underline{\mathcal{O}}}\leq\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}
at the last step. Combining with Poincaré inequality, this concludes the proof of Proposition
5.6
.
∎

5.7
Estimates for
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\eta)
and
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\underline{\eta})

The following proposition plays an essential role in section
5
.

Proposition 5.7
.

We have the following estimates:

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\eta|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},\qquad q=0,1,\\
|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\underline{\eta}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},\qquad q=0,1.\end{split}

(5.30)

Proof.

We recall the following null structure equations from Proposition
2.17
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\eta

\displaystyle=-\chi\cdot\eta+\chi\cdot\underline{\eta}-\beta,

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta}

\displaystyle=-\underline{\chi}\cdot\underline{\eta}+\underline{\chi}\cdot\eta+\underline{\beta}.

We introduce the mass aspect functions

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mu&:=-\sdiv\eta+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\chi}\cdot\widehat{}\underline{\chi}-\rho,\\
\underline{\mu}&:=-\sdiv\underline{\eta}+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{}\underline{\chi}\cdot\widehat{\chi}-\rho.\end{split}

(5.31)

By a direct computation, we obtain
14
14

14

See Lemma 4.3.1 of
[
14
]
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{4}\mu+\tr\chi\,\mu&=G+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\,\underline{\mu},\\
\nabla_{3}\underline{\mu}+\tr\underline{\chi}\,\underline{\mu}&=\underline{G}+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\mu,\end{split}

(5.32)

where

\displaystyle G=\tr\chi\,\rho+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{g},\qquad\underline{G}=\tr\underline{\chi}\,\rho+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{b}.

We introduce the following modifications of
\mu
and
\underline{\mu}
:

[\mu]:=\mu+\frac{1}{4}\tr\chi\,\tr\underline{\chi},\qquad[{\underline{\mu}}]:={\underline{\mu}}+\frac{1}{4}\tr\chi\,\tr\underline{\chi}.

(5.33)

We also define

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widecheck{[\mu]}&:=[\mu]-\overline{[\mu]}=-\sdiv\eta+\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{\chi}\cdot\widehat{}\underline{\chi}-\overline{\widehat{\chi}\cdot\widehat{}\underline{\chi}})+\frac{1}{4}\widecheck{\tr\chi\tr\underline{\chi}}-\widecheck{\rho},\\
\widecheck{[{\underline{\mu}}]}&:=[{\underline{\mu}}]-\overline{[{\underline{\mu}}]}=-\sdiv\underline{\eta}+\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{\chi}\cdot\widehat{}\underline{\chi}-\overline{\widehat{\chi}\cdot\widehat{}\underline{\chi}})+\frac{1}{4}\widecheck{\tr\chi\tr\underline{\chi}}-\widecheck{\rho},\end{split}

(5.34)

and remark that
\widecheck{[\mu]},\widecheck{[{\underline{\mu}}]}\in r^{-1}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}
.
To simplify the notations, we denote

I:=\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{\chi}\cdot\widehat{}\underline{\chi}-\overline{\widehat{\chi}\cdot\widehat{}\underline{\chi}})+\frac{1}{4}\widecheck{\tr\chi\tr\underline{\chi}}.

By the torsion equation (
2.27
) and (
5.34
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sdiv\eta&=-\widecheck{[\mu]}+I-\widecheck{\rho},\\
\curl\eta&=\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{}\underline{\chi}\wedge\widehat{\chi},\end{split}

(5.35)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sdiv\underline{\eta}&=-\widecheck{[{\underline{\mu}}]}+I-\widecheck{\rho},\\
\curl\underline{\eta}&=-\sigma-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{}\underline{\chi}\wedge\widehat{\chi}.\end{split}

(5.36)

According to (
5.31
) and applying Proposition
2.17
, we infer

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}[\mu]

\displaystyle=\nabla_{4}\left(\mu+\frac{1}{4}\tr\chi\tr\underline{\chi}\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{4}\mu+\frac{1}{4}\Omega^{-1}\tr\chi\nabla_{4}(\Omega\tr\underline{\chi})+\frac{1}{4}\nabla_{4}(\Omega^{-1}\tr\chi)\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}

\displaystyle=-\tr\chi\mu+G+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{}\underline{\chi}\cdot\widehat{\chi}-\rho-\sdiv\underline{\eta}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\tr\chi\left[-\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\tr\underline{\chi}-\widehat{\chi}\cdot\widehat{}\underline{\chi}+2\sdiv\underline{\eta}+2|\underline{\eta}|^{2}+2\rho\right]+\frac{1}{4}\tr\underline{\chi}\left[-\frac{1}{2}(\tr\chi)^{2}-|\widehat{\chi}|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=-\tr\chi[\mu]+G+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi|\underline{\eta}|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\tr\underline{\chi}|\widehat{\chi}|^{2}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}[\mu]+\tr\chi[\mu]=\tr\chi\,\rho+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{g}.

(5.37)

By Corollary
2.5
, we infer

\displaystyle\Omega\nabla_{4}\overline{[\mu]}=\overline{\widecheck{\Omega\tr\chi}\widecheck{[\mu]}}+\overline{\Omega\nabla_{4}[\mu]}=-\overline{\Omega\tr\chi[\mu]}+\overline{\Omega\tr\chi\,\rho}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{g}.

Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\widecheck{[\mu]}+\tr\chi\widecheck{[\mu]}=r^{-1}O(\widecheck{\rho})+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{g}.

(5.38)

Applying Lemma
2.30
, we deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\widecheck{[\mu]}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})\lesssim&|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\widecheck{[\mu]}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}}_{*})+\int_{{\underline{u}}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\widecheck{\rho}|_{p,S}+\int_{{\underline{u}}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{g}|_{p,S}\\
\lesssim&\frac{\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}}{r|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\int_{{\underline{u}}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}\frac{\Delta_{0}}{r^{2}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\int_{{\underline{u}}}^{{\underline{u}}_{*}}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{2}|u|^{s-2}}\\
\lesssim&\frac{\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.\end{split}

(5.39)

Applying Proposition
2.13
to (
5.35
), recalling that

\displaystyle I=\frac{1}{4}\widecheck{\tr\chi\tr\underline{\chi}}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}=r^{-1}O(\widecheck{\tr\chi})+r^{-1}O(\widecheck{\tr\underline{\chi}})+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b},

we have for
q=0,1

\displaystyle|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}(r\nabla)^{q}\eta|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\widecheck{[\mu]}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}(\widecheck{\tr\chi},\widecheck{\tr\underline{\chi}})|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})+|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\breve{\mathcal{I}}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},

where we used Propositions
5.3
,
5.4
and
5.6
. This concludes the first estimate of (
5.30
). The second estimate is similar and left to the reader. This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.7
.
∎

Remark that Propositions
5.4
and
5.7
implies (
5.2
), which concludes the first part of Theorem M3. In the sequel, we focus on the second part and hence we assume
\underline{\mathcal{O}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)\leq\mathcal{I}_{0}
.

5.8
Estimates for
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widehat{\chi})
and
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widehat{\underline{\chi}})

Proposition 5.8
.

We have the following estimate:

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\widehat{\chi}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},\qquad q=0,1,\\
|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\widehat{\underline{\chi}}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2},\qquad q=0,1.\end{split}

(5.40)

Proof.

We recall the following Codazzi equations from Proposition
2.17

\displaystyle\sdiv\widehat{\chi}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\nabla\tr\chi-\zeta\cdot\left(\widehat{\chi}-\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\right)-\beta,

\displaystyle\sdiv\widehat{\underline{\chi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\nabla\tr\underline{\chi}+\zeta\cdot\left(\widehat{\underline{\chi}}-\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right)+\underline{\beta}.

Applying Propositions
5.3
,
5.4
,
5.7
and the fact that
\zeta=\frac{\eta-\underline{\eta}}{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\sdiv\widehat{\chi}|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\tr\chi|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\zeta|_{p,S}+|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\beta|_{p,S}+|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{s-3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},

and respectively

\displaystyle|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\sdiv\widehat{\underline{\chi}}|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\tr\chi|_{p,S}+|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\zeta|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\underline{\beta}|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{s-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}}.

Combining with Proposition
2.13
, these conclude the proof of Proposition
5.8
.
∎

5.9
Estimate for
\mathcal{O}_{2}(\widecheck{\Omega})

Proposition 5.9
.

We have the following estimate for
q=0,1,2
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{1+q-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}\nabla^{q}\widecheck{\Omega}|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})&\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.\end{split}

(5.41)

Proof.

Recall that
\eta+\underline{\eta}=2\nabla\log\Omega
, which implies

\Delta\log\Omega=\frac{1}{2}\sdiv(\eta+\underline{\eta}).

Applying Propositions
2.13
and
5.7
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{{1}-\frac{2}{p}}(r\nabla)^{q}({\log\Omega})|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\sdiv(\eta+\underline{\eta})|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},\qquad q=0,1,2.\end{split}

(5.42)

Combining with (
5.18
), this concludes the proof of Proposition
5.9
.
∎

5.10
Estimate for
\left|r^{2}\left(\overline{\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}}+\frac{1}{r}\right)\right|

Proposition 5.10
.

We have the following estimate:

\displaystyle\sup\left|r^{2}\left(\overline{\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}}+\frac{1}{r}\right)\right|\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(5.43)

Proof.

We introduce the following notation:

\displaystyle\underline{W}:=\overline{\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}}+\frac{1}{r}.

As in (
5.26
), we have

\Omega\nabla_{3}\underline{W}+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}\,\underline{W}=-4\overline{{\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}(\Omega\underline{\omega})}}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{W}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b},

(5.44)

Using Lemma
2.30
and
\underline{W}\in\Gamma_{a}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\underline{W}\right|_{p,S}(u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\underline{W}\right|_{p,S}(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}})}^{u}\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{s-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{*}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r}.

Noticing that
\nabla\underline{W}=0
, taking
p=4
and applying Proposition
2.29
, we conclude the proof of Proposition
5.10
.
∎

In view of Propositions
5.1
-
5.10
, we obtain (
5.4
). This concludes the proof of Theorem M3.

6
Initialization and extension (Theorems M0, M2 and M4)

6.1
Preliminaries

6.1.1
Null frame transformations

Lemma 6.1
.

Let two null frames
(e_{3},e_{4},e_{1},e_{2})
and
(e^{\prime}_{3},e^{\prime}_{4},e^{\prime}_{1},e^{\prime}_{2})
associated to double null foliations
(u,{\underline{u}})
and
(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})
, and assume that they have the same generator
\underline{L}
for the incoming direction. Then, a null frame transformation from the null frame
(e_{3},e_{4},e_{1},e_{2})
to
(e^{\prime}_{3},e^{\prime}_{4},e^{\prime}_{1},e^{\prime}_{2})
