can be written in the form:

\displaystyle\begin{split}e^{\prime}_{4}&=\lambda\left(e_{4}+f_{B}e_{B}+\frac{1}{4}|f|^{2}e_{3}\right),\\
e^{\prime}_{3}&=\lambda^{-1}e_{3},\\
e^{\prime}_{A}&=e_{A}+\frac{1}{2}f_{A}e_{3},\end{split}

(6.1)

where

\displaystyle\lambda=\frac{\Omega}{\Omega^{\prime}}

(6.2)

is a scalar function and
f
is a
1
–form. Moreover, the inverse transform of (
6.1
) is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}e_{4}&=\lambda^{-1}e^{\prime}_{4}-f^{A}e_{A}^{\prime}+\frac{\lambda}{4}|f|^{2}e_{3}^{\prime},\\
e_{3}&=\lambda e^{\prime}_{3},\\
e_{A}&=e^{\prime}_{A}-\frac{\lambda}{2}f_{A}e^{\prime}_{3}.\end{split}

(6.3)

Proof.

Applying Lemma 3.1 in
[
19
]
with
\underline{f}=0
, we obtain (
6.1
). Notice that (
6.3
) is a direct consequence of (
6.1
). Recalling

\displaystyle e^{\prime}_{3}=2\Omega^{\prime}\underline{L},\qquad e_{3}=2\Omega\underline{L},

we obtain (
6.2
) immediately.
∎

Proposition 6.2
.

Under the null frame transform (
6.1
), some of the Ricci coefficients transform as follows:

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\tr\chi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\tr\chi+\sdiv^{\prime}f+l.o.t.,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\curl^{\prime}f+l.o.t.,

\displaystyle\lambda\underline{\chi}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\underline{\chi},

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\eta+\frac{1}{2}\lambda\nabla_{e^{\prime}_{3}}^{\prime}f-\underline{\omega}\,f+l.o.t.,

\displaystyle\underline{\eta}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\underline{\eta}+\frac{1}{4}\tr\underline{\chi}\,f+l.o.t.,

\displaystyle\lambda^{-2}\xi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\xi+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\lambda^{-1}e^{\prime}_{4}}^{\prime}f+\frac{1}{4}\tr\chi\,f+\omega f+l.o.t.,

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\omega^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\omega+l.o.t.,

where the terms denoted by
l.o.t.
have the following schematic structure

\displaystyle l.o.t.:=r^{-1}O(f^{2})+\Gamma_{b}\cdot f.

The curvature components transform as follows:

\displaystyle\lambda^{-2}\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\alpha+O(f)\beta+O(f^{2})\rho+O(f^{3})(\sigma,\underline{\beta})+O(f^{4})\underline{\alpha},

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\beta^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\beta+O(f)(\rho,\sigma)+O(f^{2})\underline{\beta}+O(f^{3})\underline{\alpha},

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\rho+O(f)\underline{\beta}+O(f^{2})\underline{\alpha},

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sigma+O(f)\underline{\beta}+O(f^{2})\underline{\alpha},

\displaystyle\lambda\underline{\beta}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\underline{\beta}+O(f)\underline{\alpha},

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}\underline{\alpha}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\underline{\alpha}.

Proof.

We only prove the transform formula of
\omega
since the others are direct consequences of Proposition 3.3 in
[
19
]
for
\underline{f}=0
. Applying (3.13) in
[
19
]
, we have

\lambda^{-1}\omega^{\prime}=\omega-\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1}e^{\prime}_{4}(\log\lambda)+\err,

where

\err=r^{-1}O(f^{2})+\Gamma_{b}\cdot f.

Applying (
6.2
), we infer

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\omega^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\omega-\frac{1}{2\lambda}e^{\prime}_{4}(\log\Omega-\log\Omega^{\prime})+\err

\displaystyle=\omega-\frac{1}{2}\left(e_{4}+f^{B}e_{B}+\frac{1}{4}|f|^{2}e_{3}\right)\log\Omega-\frac{1}{2\lambda}(2\omega^{\prime})

\displaystyle=2\omega-\lambda^{-1}\omega^{\prime}+\err,

which implies the transformation formula of
\omega
.
∎

6.1.2
Deformations of spheres

Lemma 6.3
.

Given two double null foliations
(u,{\underline{u}})
and
(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})
and we denote their leaves

S:=S(u,{\underline{u}}),\qquad S^{\prime}:=S^{\prime}(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}}).

Assume that
S\cap S^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
and the oscillation of
u
on
S^{\prime}
satisfies

\sup_{S^{\prime}}|u-\overline{u}^{\prime}|\leq\delta_{1},\qquad\quad\overline{u}^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{|S^{\prime}|}\int_{S^{\prime}}u.

(6.4)

Let
V
be a tensor field satisfying

\nabla_{\partial_{u}}V=F,

(6.5)

where for all
S

|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}F|_{L^{p}(S)}\leq\delta,\qquad p\in[1,\infty].

(6.6)

Then, we have

|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}V|_{L^{p}(S^{\prime})}\lesssim|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}V|_{L^{p}(S)}+\delta_{1}\delta.

(6.7)

Proof.

The proof is largely analogous to Lemma 4.1.7 in
[
14
]
.

Notice from (
6.5
) that

\displaystyle|\partial_{u}|V|^{2}|=|V\cdot(\nabla_{\partial_{u}}F)|\leq|V||F|,

which implies

|\partial_{u}|V||\leq|F|.

Taking
p\in S\cap S^{\prime}
, we have from (
6.4
) that

\sup_{S^{\prime}}|u-u(p)|\leq\sup_{S^{\prime}}|u-\overline{u}^{\prime}|+|u(p)-\overline{u}^{\prime}|\leq 2\delta_{1}.

(6.8)

In a sphere coordinates
\phi=(\phi^{1},\phi^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle|V|(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}},\phi)-|V|(u,{\underline{u}},\phi)=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{u}(|V|)(u+t(u^{\prime}-u),{\underline{u}},\phi)(u^{\prime}-u)dt

(6.9)

Hence, we infer

\displaystyle|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}V|_{L^{p}(S^{\prime})}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-2}|V|^{p}(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}},\phi)\sqrt{\det(g)|_{S^{\prime}}}\,d\phi^{1}d\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|V|^{p}(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}},\phi)d\phi^{1}d\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|V|^{p}(u,{\underline{u}},\phi)d\phi^{1}d\phi^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}|F|(u+t(u^{\prime}-u),{\underline{u}},\phi)|u^{\prime}-u|dt\right)^{p}d\phi^{1}d\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}V|^{p}\sqrt{\det(g)|_{S}}\,d\phi^{1}d\phi^{2}+\delta_{1}^{p}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}|F|(u+t(u^{\prime}-u),{\underline{u}},\phi)dt\right)^{p}d\phi^{1}d\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}V|_{L^{p}(S)}^{p}+\delta_{1}^{p}|F(u+t(u^{\prime}-u),{\underline{u}},\phi)|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{S}^{2})L^{1}_{t}(0,1)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}V|_{L^{p}(S)}^{p}+\delta_{1}^{p}|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}F|_{L^{1}_{t}(0,1)L^{p}(S(u+t(u^{\prime}-u),{\underline{u}}))}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}V|_{L^{p}(S)}^{p}+\delta_{1}^{p}\delta^{p},

where we used (
6.6
), (
6.8
), (
6.9
) and General Minkowski inequality. This concludes the proof of Lemma
6.3
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

In the remainder of this paper, for any quantity
X
, we denote the quantity
X_{(0)}
associated to the initial data layer region
\mathcal{K}_{(0)}
by

\displaystyle X^{\prime}:=X_{(0)},

to simplify the notations. For example:

\displaystyle u^{\prime}:=u_{(0)},\qquad\eta^{\prime}:=\eta_{(0)},\qquad\mathcal{O}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{O}_{(0)},\qquad\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}:=\mathfrak{R}_{(0)},\qquad S^{\prime}:=S_{(0)},\qquad\mathcal{K}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{K}_{(0)}.

6.2
The initial hypersurface (Theorem M0)

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem M0 which we recall below for convenience.

Theorem M0
.

Under the assumptions

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\leq\epsilon_{0},\qquad\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}\leq\epsilon_{0},\qquad\mathcal{O}\leq\epsilon,\qquad\osc\leq\epsilon,

(6.10)

we have

\mathfrak{R}_{0}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\breve{\underline{\mathcal{O}}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.11)

If in addition we assume that

\breve{\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

(6.12)

then, we have

\osc\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\underline{\mathcal{O}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.13)

Recall that we denoted by
(f,\lambda)
the frame transformation from
(u,{\underline{u}})
to
(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})
, and let
(f^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})
its inverse transformation. Notice from (
6.1
) and (
6.3
) that we have

\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda^{-1},\qquad f^{\prime}=-\lambda f.

(6.14)

Remark 6.5
.

In
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
, we have
|u|\simeq r
. Hence,
\Gamma_{b}
has the same decay as
\Gamma_{g}
in
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
. The assumption
\osc\leq\epsilon
implies that
r\simeq r^{\prime}
and
f\in r\Gamma_{g}
.

We first prove (
6.11
) under the assumptions in (
6.10
).

Lemma 6.6
.

Under the assumptions

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\leq\epsilon_{0},\qquad\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}\leq\epsilon_{0},\qquad\mathcal{O}\leq\epsilon,\qquad\osc\leq\epsilon,

we have

\mathfrak{R}_{0}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}=\int_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}\sum_{l=0}^{1}{r}^{s}\left(|{\mathfrak{d}}^{l}\alpha|^{2}+|{\mathfrak{d}}^{l}\beta|^{2}+|{\mathfrak{d}}^{l}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)|^{2}+|{\mathfrak{d}}^{l}\underline{\beta}|^{2}+|{\mathfrak{d}}^{l}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}\right)+\sup_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}|{r}^{3}\overline{\rho}|^{2},

Applying Proposition
6.2
with (
6.14
), we infer
15
15

15

Recall that
{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}f=O(\epsilon)r^{-\frac{s-1}{2}}
and
\rho^{\prime}=O(\epsilon)r^{-3}
.

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}\alpha|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}(\lambda^{-2}\alpha^{\prime})|^{2}+r^{s}\left|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}(\lambda^{-1}f\cdot\beta^{\prime})\right|^{2}+r^{s}\left|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}(f^{2}\cdot\rho^{\prime})\right|^{2}+l.o.t.

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}}^{2}+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}(f^{2})\rho^{\prime}|^{2}+r^{s}|f^{2}{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}\rho^{\prime}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}}^{2}+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}r^{s}\left|\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{r^{s+2}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{2}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}\beta|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}(\lambda^{-1}\beta^{\prime})|^{2}+r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}f\cdot(\rho^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}}^{2}+\int_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}r^{s}\left|\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{\frac{s+5}{2}}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{2}.

By the same method, we obtain:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\setminus K}r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}\widecheck{\rho}|^{2}+r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}\sigma|^{2}+r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}\underline{\beta}|^{2}+r^{s}|{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}\underline{\alpha}|^{2}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{2}.

Finally, we estimate

\displaystyle|r^{3}\overline{\rho}|\lesssim|r^{3}\rho|_{\infty}\lesssim|r^{3}\rho^{\prime}|_{\infty}+|r^{4}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\beta}^{\prime}|_{\infty}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
6.6
.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

Under the assumptions

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\leq\epsilon_{0},\qquad\mathcal{O}\leq\epsilon,\qquad\osc\leq\epsilon,

we have

\breve{\underline{\mathcal{O}}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Proof.

We recall from Proposition
6.2
that
16
16

16

Recall that
\Gamma_{b}=\Gamma_{g}
in
\mathcal{K}_{(0)}
and
f\in r\Gamma_{g}
.

\displaystyle{\Omega^{\prime}}^{-1}\tr\underline{\chi}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Omega^{-1}\tr\underline{\chi}+r\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g},

\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Omega\omega+r\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega\omega|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}+|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{2}\Omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}|_{\infty,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{s-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Notice from (
6.1
) and Proposition
2.17
that

\displaystyle re_{A}(\Omega\omega)

\displaystyle=re_{A}^{\prime}(\Omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime})-\frac{\lambda}{2}rf_{A}e^{\prime}_{3}(\Omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime})+r\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=re_{A}^{\prime}(\Omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime})+O(r\rho^{\prime})f+r\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

We deduce

\displaystyle|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla(\Omega\omega)|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla^{\prime}\Omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\Gamma_{a}|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r\nabla^{\prime}\Omega^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}|_{\infty,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla(\Omega^{-1}\tr\underline{\chi})|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}.

Applying Poincaré inequality and recalling the definition of
\breve{\underline{\mathcal{O}}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)
, we obtain

\breve{\underline{\mathcal{O}}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)=\sum_{q=0}^{1}\left(\underline{\mathcal{O}}_{q}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)(\widecheck{\Omega^{-1}\tr\underline{\chi}})+\underline{\mathcal{O}}_{q}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)(\widecheck{\Omega\omega})\right)+\underline{\mathcal{O}}_{0}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)(\Omega\omega)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
6.7
.
∎

Remark that Lemmas
6.6
and
6.7
imply (
6.11
), which concludes the first part of Theorem M0. We now focus on the second part of Theorem M0 under the additional assumption (
6.12
).

Lemma 6.8

(Oscillation lemma)
.

Under the assumptions:

\mathcal{O}_{(0)}\leq\epsilon_{0},\qquad\mathcal{O}\leq\epsilon,\qquad\osc\leq\epsilon,\qquad\breve{\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

(6.15)

we have

\osc\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.16)

Proof.

Step 1.
Estimate for
\osc(f)
.

We have from Proposition
6.2
and Remark
6.5

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\tr\chi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\tr\chi+\sdiv^{\prime}f+f\cdot(\Gamma_{g},r^{-1}f),

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\curl^{\prime}f+f\cdot(\Gamma_{g},r^{-1}f),

which implies from (
6.2
) and the fact that
f\in r\Gamma_{g}
17
17

17

We have from (
6.1
) and Proposition
6.2
that
\nabla^{\prime}f-\nabla f=O(f)\cdot\nabla_{3}f=f\cdot\Gamma_{g}
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}&=\Omega\tr\chi+\Omega\sdiv f+f\cdot\Gamma_{g},\\
0&=\curl f+f\cdot\Gamma_{g}.\end{split}

(6.17)

Differentiating it by
e_{A}
and applying (
6.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle e_{A}^{\prime}(\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime})-\frac{\lambda}{2}f_{A}e^{\prime}_{3}(\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime})=e_{A}(\Omega\tr\chi)+\Omega e_{A}(\sdiv f)+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

Recall that we have from Proposition
2.17

\displaystyle e_{3}^{\prime}(\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}\tr\underline{\chi}^{\prime}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{a}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{a}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\Omega e_{A}(\sdiv f)+\frac{1}{2r^{2}}f_{A}=e^{\prime}_{A}(\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime})-e_{A}(\Omega\tr\chi)+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)},

which implies

\displaystyle e_{A}(\sdiv f)+\frac{1}{r^{2}}f_{A}=\Omega^{-1}(e^{\prime}_{A}(\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime})-e_{A}(\Omega\tr\chi))+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

We also have from (
6.17
)

\displaystyle e_{A}(\curl f)=\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

Hence, we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(d_{1}^{*}d_{1}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)f&=-\nabla\sdiv f+{{}^{*}\nabla}\curl f-\frac{1}{r^{2}}f\\
&=-\Omega^{-1}\nabla^{\prime}(\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime})+\Omega^{-1}\nabla(\Omega\tr\chi)+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.\end{split}

(6.18)

Recall from (
2.23
) and Corollary
2.20
that

\displaystyle d_{1}^{*}d_{1}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\Delta_{1}+\mathbf{K}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\Delta_{1}-\frac{1}{4}\tr\chi\,\tr\underline{\chi}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}-\rho-\frac{1}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\Delta_{1}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{a}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

Injecting it into (
6.18
), we deduce

\displaystyle-\Delta_{1}f=-\Omega^{-1}\nabla^{\prime}(\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime})+\Omega^{-1}\nabla(\Omega\tr\chi)+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

We have from Proposition
2.13

\displaystyle|r^{\frac{s-1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}(r\nabla)^{\leq 2}f|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{\frac{s+3}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}(\nabla^{\prime}\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime},\nabla\Omega\tr\chi)|_{p,S}+|r^{\frac{s+3}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{p,S}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Applying Proposition
2.29
, we deduce

|r^{\frac{s-1}{2}}(r\nabla)^{\leq 1}f|_{\infty,S}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.19)

We have from Proposition
6.2
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|\nabla_{3}f|&\lesssim\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|\eta^{\prime}-\eta|+\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|f\cdot\Gamma_{a}|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{\frac{s+3}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}},\\
\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|\nabla_{4}f|&\lesssim\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|r^{-1}f|+\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|f\cdot\Gamma_{a}|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{\frac{s+3}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}.\end{split}

(6.20)

Combining (
6.19
) and (
6.20
), we deduce

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}(|f|+|{\mathfrak{d}}f|)\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s-1}{2}}},

which implies

\osc(f)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.21)

Step 2.
Estimate for
\osc(\lambda)
.

We recall from (
6.2
) that

\displaystyle|r\overset{\circ}{\lambda}|=|r(\lambda-1)|\lesssim r|\Omega^{\prime}-\Omega|\lesssim r\left|\Omega^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}-\Omega\right|\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

where we used (
6.15
) in the last step. Hence, we obtain

\osc(\lambda)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.22)

Step 3.
Estimate for
\osc(r)
.

We have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}&=\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}-\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}-\Omega\tr\chi+\frac{1}{r}+(\Omega\tr\chi-\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime})\\
&=\widecheck{\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}}-\widecheck{\Omega\tr\chi}+\overline{\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}+\overline{\Omega\tr\chi}-\frac{1}{r}+(\Omega\tr\chi-\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}).\end{split}

(6.23)

Applying (
6.17
) and (
6.19
), we deduce

\displaystyle|\Omega\tr\chi-\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}|\lesssim|\sdiv f|+|r\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}.

(6.24)

Moreover, we have from (
6.15
) that

\displaystyle r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}\left(|\widecheck{\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}}|+|\widecheck{\Omega\tr\chi}|\right)+r^{2}\left(\left|\overline{\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}\right|+\left|\overline{\Omega\tr\chi}-\frac{1}{r}\right|\right)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.25)

Hence, we obtain from (
6.23
), (
6.24
) and (
6.25
) that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}\right|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{2}},

which implies

\osc(r)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.26)

Combining (
6.21
), (
6.22
) and (
6.26
), we infer

\osc\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.27)

This concludes the proof of Lemma
6.8
.
∎

Proof of Theorem M0.

We have from Lemma
6.8
that

\osc\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.28)

We recall from Proposition
6.2
that

\displaystyle\underline{\eta}^{\prime}=\underline{\eta}+\frac{1}{4}\tr\underline{\chi}f+r\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}.

Applying (
6.1
), we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}\underline{\eta}^{\prime}=\nabla\underline{\eta}+r^{-1}O(\nabla f)+r\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

Thus, we obtain from (
6.10
) and (
6.28
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|\underline{\eta}|\lesssim\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|\underline{\eta}^{\prime}|+\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|r^{-1}f|+\sup_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}|r\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}},

(6.29)

and for
p\in[2,4]

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\underline{\eta}|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}&\lesssim|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla^{\prime}\underline{\eta}^{\prime}|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}+|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla f|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{p,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}\\
&\lesssim|r\nabla^{\prime}\underline{\eta}^{\prime}|_{\infty,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}+|\nabla f|_{\infty,S(u_{0}({\underline{u}}),{\underline{u}})}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{s-1}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}.\end{split}

(6.30)

Finally, we recall from Proposition
6.2
that

\displaystyle\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}=\frac{\Omega^{2}}{\Omega^{\prime}}\tr\underline{\chi}^{\prime}.

Hence, we infer

\displaystyle\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{r}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Omega^{2}}{\Omega^{\prime}}\tr\underline{\chi}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{r}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\Omega^{2}}{{\Omega^{\prime}}^{2}}\right)\tr\underline{\chi}^{\prime}+\Omega^{\prime}\tr\underline{\chi}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}.

Thus, we have from (
6.10
) and (
6.28
)

\displaystyle r^{2}\left|\Omega\tr\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{r}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{2}\left|\Omega^{\prime}\tr\underline{\chi}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}\right|+r|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}|+|r^{\prime}-r|\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.31)

Combining (
6.29
), (
6.30
), (
6.31
) and Lemma
6.7
and recalling the definition of
\underline{\mathcal{O}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)
, we obtain

\underline{\mathcal{O}}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus K)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.32)

This concludes the proof of Theorem M0.
∎

6.3
The last slice (Theorem M2)

We now assume that the last slice
{\underline{C}}_{*}
is endowed with a geodesic foliation. Notice that
\Omega=\frac{1}{2}
and
\underline{\omega}=0
on
{\underline{C}}_{*}
. We write the null structure equations along the last slice in the following form:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{3}\zeta+\tr\underline{\chi}\,\zeta+2\widehat{\underline{\chi}}\cdot\zeta&=-\underline{\beta},\\
\nabla_{3}\tr\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}(\tr\underline{\chi})^{2}+|\widehat{}\underline{\chi}|^{2}&=0,\\
\nabla_{3}\widehat{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\widehat{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\,\widehat{}\underline{\chi}-\nabla\widehat{\otimes}\zeta-\zeta\widehat{\otimes}\zeta&=0,\\
\nabla_{3}\tr\chi+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\tr\chi+\widehat{}\underline{\chi}\cdot\widehat{\chi}-2\sdiv\zeta-2|\zeta|^{2}&=2\rho.\end{split}

(6.33)

We also have the Codazzi equations:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sdiv\widehat{\chi}&=\frac{1}{2}\nabla\tr\chi-\zeta\left(\widehat{\chi}-\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\right)-\beta,\\
\sdiv\widehat{}\underline{\chi}&=\frac{1}{2}\nabla\tr\underline{\chi}+\zeta\left(\widehat{}\underline{\chi}-\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right)+\underline{\beta}.\end{split}

(6.34)

For convenience, we recall the statement of Theorem M2.

Theorem M2
.

Let
{\underline{C}}_{*}
endowed with a geodesic foliation. We assume that

\mathcal{O}^{*}({\underline{C}}_{*})\leq\epsilon,\qquad\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}\leq\Delta_{0},\qquad\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{I}_{0}.

(6.35)

Then, we have

\mathcal{O}^{*}({\underline{C}}_{*})\lesssim\Delta_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(6.36)

In the remainder of this section, we always assume
p\in[2,4]
and we denote

S:=S(u,{\underline{u}}_{*}),\qquad S_{*}:={\underline{C}}_{*}\cap\Sigma_{0}.

(6.37)

Proof of Theorem M2.

Notice that the assumption
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{I}_{0}
controls the initial data on the last sphere
S_{*}:={\underline{C}}_{*}\cap\Sigma_{0}
. The idea of the proof is to transport the estimates in the direction of
\nabla_{3}
along
{\underline{C}}_{*}
using (
6.33
). The proof is similar to section
5
, and in fact easier since
\Omega=\frac{1}{2}
on
{\underline{C}}_{*}
. We only provide a sketch.

Step 1.
Estimate for
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{0,1,2}(\widecheck{\tr\underline{\chi}})
.

The estimates for
\mathcal{O}_{0}^{*}(\tr\underline{\chi})
and
\mathcal{O}_{1}^{*}(\tr\underline{\chi})
follow directly from the results of section
5.6
in the particular case
\Omega=\frac{1}{2}
and
\underline{\omega}=0
.

We then estimate
\nabla^{2}\tr\underline{\chi}
. According to the assumption
\mathcal{O}^{*}({\underline{C}}_{*})\leq\epsilon
, we have on
{\underline{C}}_{*}
:

r\nabla\tr\underline{\chi}\in\Gamma_{g},\qquad(r\nabla)^{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\in\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

(6.38)

We recall from (
6.33
) that

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\tr\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}(\tr\underline{\chi})^{2}=-|\widehat{\underline{\chi}}|^{2}.

Differentiating it by
r^{2}\Delta
and applying Proposition
2.24
, we deduce

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(r^{2}\Delta\tr\underline{\chi})+\tr\underline{\chi}(r^{2}\Delta\tr\underline{\chi})=-r^{2}\Delta|\widehat{\underline{\chi}}|^{2}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.

(6.39)

We recall from (
6.34
) that

\sdiv\widehat{\underline{\chi}}=\frac{1}{2}\nabla\tr\underline{\chi}-\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\zeta+\underline{\beta}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}=\underline{\beta}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{g}.

(6.40)

Applying Proposition
2.13
, we obtain
18
18

18

Here,
\underline{\beta}^{(0)}
denotes a quantity which has same or even better decay and regularity than
\underline{\beta}
.

\displaystyle\nabla\widehat{\underline{\chi}}=\underline{\beta}^{(0)}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{g}.

Hence, we have

|\nabla\widehat{\underline{\chi}}|^{2}=\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot\underline{\beta}^{(0)}+r^{-2}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.

Moreover, we have from (
2.23
) and (
6.40
)

\displaystyle 2\Delta_{2}\widehat{\underline{\chi}}\cdot\widehat{\underline{\chi}}=4((\mathbf{K}-d_{2}^{*}d_{2})\widehat{\underline{\chi}})\cdot\widehat{\underline{\chi}}=-4d_{2}^{*}d_{2}\widehat{\underline{\chi}}\cdot\widehat{\underline{\chi}}+r^{-2}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}=-4(d_{2}^{*}\underline{\beta})\cdot\widehat{\underline{\chi}}+r^{-2}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.

We then compute

\displaystyle\Delta|\widehat{\underline{\chi}}|^{2}=2\nabla^{C}\left((\nabla_{C}\widehat{\underline{\chi}}_{AB})\widehat{\underline{\chi}}^{AB}\right)=2(\Delta_{2}\widehat{\underline{\chi}})\cdot\widehat{\underline{\chi}}+2|\nabla\widehat{\underline{\chi}}|^{2}=-4(d_{2}^{*}\underline{\beta})\cdot\widehat{\underline{\chi}}+\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot\underline{\beta}^{(0)}+r^{-2}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.

Injecting it into (
6.39
), we deduce

\nabla_{3}(r^{2}\Delta\tr\underline{\chi})+\tr\underline{\chi}(r^{2}\Delta\tr\underline{\chi})=4r^{2}(d_{2}^{*}\underline{\beta})\cdot\widehat{\underline{\chi}}+r^{2}\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot\underline{\beta}^{(0)}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.

(6.41)

Applying Proposition
2.13
, we have

\displaystyle|d_{2}^{*}\underline{\beta}|_{p,S}\lesssim|d_{1}\underline{\beta}|_{p,S},

which implies that there exists a bounded elliptic operator
A:L^{p}(S)\to L^{p}(S)
of order
0
such that

\displaystyle d_{2}^{*}\underline{\beta}=A(d_{1}\underline{\beta}).

We have from Proposition
2.23

\displaystyle\begin{split}[\Omega\nabla_{3},A]&=[\Omega\nabla_{3},rd_{2}^{*}\circ(rd_{1})^{-1}]=[\Omega\nabla_{3},rd_{2}^{*}]\circ(rd_{1})^{-1}+rd_{2}^{*}\circ[\Omega\nabla_{3},(rd_{1})^{-1}]\\
&=\Gamma_{b}\cdot rd_{2}^{*}\circ(rd_{1})^{-1}+r\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot(rd_{1})^{-1}-rd_{2}^{*}\circ(rd_{1})^{-1}\circ[\Omega\nabla_{3},rd_{1}]\circ(rd_{1})^{-1}\\
&=\Gamma_{b}\cdot A+r\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot(rd_{1})^{-1}-A\circ\left(\Gamma_{b}\cdot rd_{1}+r\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\right)\circ(rd_{1})^{-1}\\
&=\Gamma_{b}+r\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot(rd_{1})^{-1}.\end{split}

(6.42)

We recall from Corollary
2.20
and Lemma
2.5
that

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})+\frac{3}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})=-d_{1}\underline{\beta}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\alpha}.

Combining with (
6.42
), we deduce

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(r^{2}A(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma}))=r(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})^{(0)}-A(r^{2}d_{1}\underline{\beta})+r^{3}\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})^{(0)}+r^{2}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\alpha}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle 4\widehat{\underline{\chi}}\cdot(r^{2}d_{2}^{*}\underline{\beta})=-4\nabla_{3}(r^{2}A(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})\widehat{\underline{\chi}})+r^{2}\underline{\alpha}^{(0)}\cdot(\widecheck{\rho}^{(0)},\widecheck{\sigma}^{(0)})+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.

Injecting it into (
6.41
), we infer
19
19

19

Note that
\underline{\alpha}^{(0)}\cdot(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})^{(0)}
has the same decay as
\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot\underline{\beta}^{(0)}
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{3}\left(r^{2}\Delta\tr\underline{\chi}+4r^{2}A(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})\widehat{\underline{\chi}}\right)+\tr\underline{\chi}\left(r^{2}\Delta\tr\underline{\chi}+4r^{2}A(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})\widehat{\underline{\chi}}\right)=r^{2}\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot\underline{\beta}^{(0)}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.\end{split}

(6.43)

Applying Lemma
2.30
, we have

\displaystyle\;\;\;\,\,\,\left|r^{4-\frac{2}{p}}(\Delta\tr\underline{\chi}+4A(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})\widehat{\underline{\chi}})\right|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|r^{4-\frac{2}{p}}(\Delta\tr\underline{\chi}+4A(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})\widehat{\underline{\chi}})\right|_{p,S_{*}}+\int^{u}_{u_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})}|r^{4-\frac{2}{p}}\underline{\beta}^{(0)}\cdot\underline{\beta}^{(0)}|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\int^{u}_{u_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})}|r^{2-\frac{2}{8}}\underline{\beta}^{(0)}|_{8,S}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{s-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})}^{u}|r^{2-\frac{2}{2}}\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|_{2,S}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},

where we used the Sobolev inequality

\displaystyle|r^{-\frac{2}{8}}\underline{\beta}^{(0)}|_{8,S}\lesssim|r^{-\frac{2}{2}}\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|_{2,S}.

Hence, we obtain from Proposition
2.13

\left|r^{4-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla^{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right|_{p,S}\lesssim\left|r^{4-\frac{2}{p}}\Delta\tr\underline{\chi}\right|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\left|r^{4-\frac{2}{p}}A(\widecheck{\rho},\widecheck{\sigma})\widehat{\underline{\chi}}\right|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

(6.44)

Hence, we obtain

\mathcal{O}^{*}_{0}(\widecheck{\tr\underline{\chi}})+\mathcal{O}^{*}_{1}(\widecheck{\tr\underline{\chi}})+\mathcal{O}^{*}_{2}(\widecheck{\tr\underline{\chi}})\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(6.45)

Step 2.
Estimate for
\widecheck{[{\underline{\mu}}]}
.

We introduce the mass aspect function
{\underline{\mu}}
as in (

\displaystyle{\underline{\mu}}:=\sdiv\zeta+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\underline{\chi}}\cdot\widehat{\chi}-\rho.

(6.46)

We recall from Lemma 4.3.1 in

that

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}{\underline{\mu}}+\tr\underline{\chi}\,{\underline{\mu}}=\underline{G}+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}{\underline{\mu}},

(6.47)

(6.48)

Differentiating (

(6.49)

Recall from (

) and Corollary

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\widehat{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\widehat{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\,\widehat{\underline{\chi}}

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)+\frac{3}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)

\displaystyle=-d_{1}\underline{\beta}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\alpha}.

We apply a renormalization method similar to the one in (

\nabla_{3}\Xi+\tr\underline{\chi}\,\Xi=O(\nabla\rho)+r^{-1}\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)},

(6.50)

where

(6.51)

Next, we use a renormalization method similar to the one in section
5.2
. We define
{\underline{\mu}}^{\dagger}
by:

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}{\underline{\mu}}^{\dagger}+\tr\underline{\chi}\,{\underline{\mu}}^{\dagger}=\tr\underline{\chi}\,\sigma\quad\mbox{ on }{\underline{C}}_{*},\qquad{\underline{\mu}}^{\dagger}=0\quad\mbox{ on }S_{*}.

(6.52)

(6.53)

which will be improved in (

) and (
6.59

(6.55)

=&r^{-1}O(\beta)+r^{-1}\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.\end{split}

Applying Lemma

, we deduce

|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}(\Xi+r{{}^{*}\nabla}{\underline{\mu}}^{\dagger}-r\tr\underline{\chi}\beta)|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

(6.57)

Recall from (

we obtain

|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}(r\nabla\widecheck{[{\underline{\mu}}]}+r{{}^{*}\nabla}{\underline{\mu}}^{\dagger})|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\beta|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{p,S}+\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

Then, we have from Proposition
2.13
that

\displaystyle\left|r^{4-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\left([{\underline{\mu}}],{\underline{\mu}}^{\dagger}\right)\right|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

(6.58)

Finally, applying Lemma
2.30
to (

|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}{\underline{\mu}}^{\dagger}|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}{\underline{\mu}}^{\dagger}|_{p,S_{*}}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})}^{u}|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\sigma|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}}{r|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

(6.59)

Remark that (

Step 3.
Estimates for
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widecheck{\tr\chi})
and
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\zeta)
.

We recall from (

) that

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\tr\chi+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\tr\chi-2\sdiv\zeta=2\rho+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}.

Applying (
6.46
) and (
6.47
), we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\tr\chi+\tr\underline{\chi}\tr\chi

\displaystyle=2\sdiv\zeta+2\rho+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\tr\underline{\chi}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}

\displaystyle=2{\underline{\mu}}+4\rho+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\tr\underline{\chi}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}

\displaystyle=2[{\underline{\mu}}]+4\rho+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}.

Differentiating it by
r\nabla
, we deduce

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(r\nabla\tr\chi)+\tr\underline{\chi}(r\nabla\tr\chi)

\displaystyle=-\tr\chi(r\nabla\tr\underline{\chi})+2r\nabla[{\underline{\mu}}]+4r\nabla\rho+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

We use a renormalization argument as in Step 2. For this, we define
\chi^{\dagger}
by:

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\chi^{\dagger}+\tr\underline{\chi}\,\chi^{\dagger}=4\sigma,\quad\mbox{ on }{\underline{C}}_{*},\qquad\chi^{\dagger}=0,\quad\mbox{ on }S_{*}.

(6.60)

We add the bootstrap assumption

\chi^{\dagger}\in\Gamma_{g},

(6.61)

which will be improved by (
6.62
) and (
6.63
). Differentiating it by
r{{}^{*}\nabla}
and applying Corollary
2.22
, we infer

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(r{{}^{*}\nabla}\chi^{\dagger})=4r{{}^{*}\nabla}\sigma+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

We recall from Corollary
2.20
that

\nabla_{3}\beta+\tr\underline{\chi}\,\beta=\nabla\widecheck{\rho}+{{}^{*}\nabla\sigma}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\underline{\beta},

which implies

\nabla_{3}(r\beta)+\tr\underline{\chi}\,(r\beta)=-e_{3}(r)\beta+r\nabla\widecheck{\rho}+r{{}^{*}\nabla\sigma}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}^{(1)}.

Hence, we obtain the following analog of (

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(r\nabla\tr\chi+r{{}^{*}\nabla}\chi^{\dagger}-4r\beta)+\tr\underline{\chi}(r\nabla\tr\chi+r{{}^{*}\nabla}\chi^{\dagger}-4r\beta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e_{3}(r)\beta-\tr\chi(r\nabla\tr\underline{\chi})+2r\nabla[{\underline{\mu}}]+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}.

Applying Lemma
2.30
, (
6.45
) and (
6.58
), we deduce

\displaystyle|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}(r\nabla\tr\chi+r{{}^{*}\nabla}\chi^{\dagger}-4r\beta)|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}(r\nabla\tr\chi+r{{}^{*}\nabla}\chi^{\dagger}-4r\beta)|_{p,S_{*}}

\displaystyle+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})}^{u}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\beta|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\tr\underline{\chi}|_{p,S}+|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla[{\underline{\mu}}]|_{p,S}

\displaystyle+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})}^{u}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{a}\cdot\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})}^{u}\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{s-2}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{2}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

Hence, we obtain from Proposition
2.13
that

\displaystyle|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla(\tr\chi,\chi^{\dagger})|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\beta|_{p,S}+\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

(6.62)

Applying Lemma
2.30
to (
6.60
), we easily obtain

|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\chi^{\dagger}|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\chi^{\dagger}|_{p,S_{*}}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})}^{u}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\sigma|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},

(6.63)

which improved (
6.61
). By Poincaré inequality, we infer

\mathcal{O}_{0}(\widecheck{\tr\chi})+\mathcal{O}_{1}(\widecheck{\tr\chi})\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(6.64)

Next, we recall from (
5.36
) that

\displaystyle\sdiv\zeta

\displaystyle=\widecheck{[{\underline{\mu}}]}+\widecheck{\rho}+\frac{1}{4}\widecheck{\tr\chi\,\tr\underline{\chi}}+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g},

\displaystyle\curl\zeta

\displaystyle=\sigma+\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}.

Hence, we have from Proposition
2.13
that

\displaystyle|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}(r\nabla)^{\leq 1}\zeta|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\widecheck{[{\underline{\mu}}]}|_{p,S}+|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)|_{p,S}+|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}(\widecheck{\tr\chi},\widecheck{\tr\underline{\chi}})|_{p,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},

where we used (
6.45
), (
6.58
) and (
6.64
) in the last step. Hence, we obtain

\mathcal{O}_{0}(\zeta)+\mathcal{O}_{1}(\zeta)\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(6.65)

Step 4.
Estimates for
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widehat{\chi})
and
\mathcal{O}_{0,1}(\widehat{\underline{\chi}})
.

Applying Proposition
2.13
to (

\displaystyle|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}(r\nabla)^{\leq 1}\widehat{\chi}|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\widecheck{\tr\chi}|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\zeta|_{p,S}+|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}\beta|+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{g}|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},

\displaystyle|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}(r\nabla)^{\leq 1}\widehat{\underline{\chi}}|_{p,S}\lesssim|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla\widecheck{\tr\underline{\chi}}|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\zeta|_{p,S}+|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\underline{\beta}|+|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{b}\cdot\Gamma_{g}|_{p,S}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}}.

Hence, we have

\mathcal{O}_{0}(\widehat{\chi})+\mathcal{O}_{1}(\widehat{\chi})+\mathcal{O}_{0}(\widehat{\underline{\chi}})+\mathcal{O}_{1}(\widehat{\underline{\chi}})\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(6.66)

Step 5.
Estimate for
r^{2}\left|\overline{\tr\chi}-\frac{2}{r}\right|
.

We have from Corollary
2.5
and (
6.33
) that
20
20

20

Recall that
\Omega=\frac{1}{2}
on
{\underline{C}}_{*}
.

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}(\overline{\tr\chi})=\overline{\nabla_{3}(\tr\chi)}+\overline{\widecheck{\tr\underline{\chi}}\,\widecheck{\tr\chi}}=-\frac{1}{2}\overline{\tr\chi\,\tr\underline{\chi}}+2\overline{\rho}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}.

Notice that

\nabla_{3}\left(\frac{2}{r}\right)=-\frac{1}{r}\overline{\tr\underline{\chi}}.

Hence, we infer from Lemma
2.3
that

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\left(\overline{\tr\chi}-\frac{2}{r}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\left(\overline{\tr\chi}-\frac{2}{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\left(\overline{\tr\chi}-\frac{2}{r}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\overline{\tr\chi\,\tr\underline{\chi}}+\frac{1}{r}\overline{\tr\underline{\chi}}+2\overline{\rho}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot\Gamma_{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\overline{\tr\underline{\chi}}\left(\overline{\tr\chi}-\frac{2}{r}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\overline{\tr\chi}\,\overline{\tr\underline{\chi}}+\frac{1}{r}\overline{\tr\underline{\chi}}+2\overline{\rho}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})

\displaystyle=2\overline{\rho}+\Gamma_{g}\cdot(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b}).

Applying Lemma
2.30
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|r\left(\overline{\tr\chi}-\frac{2}{r}\right)\right|_{\infty,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|r\left(\overline{\tr\chi}-\frac{2}{r}\right)\right|_{\infty,S_{*}}+\int_{u_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})}^{u}|r\overline{\rho}|_{\infty,S}+|r\Gamma_{g}\cdot(\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b})|_{\infty,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{I}_{0}}{r}+\int_{u_{0}}^{u}\frac{\Delta_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\Delta_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{0}+\epsilon^{2}}{r}.

Hence, we deduce

\sup_{{\underline{C}}_{*}}\left|r^{2}\left(\overline{\tr\chi}-\frac{2}{r}\right)\right|\lesssim\Delta_{0}+\mathcal{I}_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

(6.67)

In view of (
6.45
), (
6.64
)–(
6.67
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{*}({\underline{C}}_{*})\lesssim\mathcal{I}_{0}+\Delta_{0}+\epsilon^{2}.

This concludes the proof of Theorem M2.
∎

6.4
Extension argument (Theorem M4)

In this section, we prove Theorem M4, which we recall below for convenience.

Theorem M4
.

We consider the spacetime
\mathcal{K}
and its double null foliation
(u,{\underline{u}})
, which satisfy the assumptions:

\mathcal{O}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\mathcal{R}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\osc\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.68)

Then, we can extend the spacetime
\mathcal{K}=V(u_{0},{\underline{u}}_{*})
and the double null foliation
(u,{\underline{u}})
to a new spacetime
\wideparen{\mathcal{K}}=\wideparen{V}(u_{0},{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu)
, where
\nu
is sufficiently small, and an associated double null foliation
(\wideparen{u},{\underline{u}})
. Moreover, the new foliation
(\wideparen{u},{\underline{u}})
is geodesic on the new last slice
{\underline{C}}_{**}:={\underline{C}}_{{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu}
and
the new norms satisfy

\displaystyle\wideparen{\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\wideparen{\mathcal{R}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\wideparen{\osc}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.69)

Recall that we have introduced three different double null foliations in different regions:

•

(u,{\underline{u}})
in the bootstrap region
\mathcal{K}
;

•

(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})
in the initial layer region
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
;

•

(\wideparen{u},{\underline{u}})
in the extended bootstrap region
\wideparen{\mathcal{K}}
.

We introduce the following definition.

Definition 6.9
.

For the change of frame
(f,\lambda)
from a double null foliation
(u^{(1)},{\underline{u}})
to another double null foliation
(u^{(2)},{\underline{u}})
in their common domain
\mathcal{M}
, we define the following norms:

\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](\mathcal{M}):=\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](\mathcal{M})(f)+\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](\mathcal{M})(\lambda)+\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](\mathcal{M})(r),

where

\displaystyle\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](f)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathcal{M}}\left|r^{(1)}|u^{(1)}|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}{\mathfrak{d}}^{\leq 1}f\right|,

\displaystyle\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](\lambda)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathcal{M}}\left|r^{(1)}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\right|,

\displaystyle\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](r)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathcal{M}}\left|r^{(1)}-r^{(2)}\right|.

Remark 6.10
.

Definition
6.9
immediately implies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(3)}](\mathcal{M})&\lesssim\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](\mathcal{M})+\osc[u^{(2)},u^{(3)}](\mathcal{M}),\\
\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](\mathcal{M}_{1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{2})&\lesssim\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](\mathcal{M}_{1})+\osc[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}](\mathcal{M}_{2}).\end{split}

(6.70)

To simplify the notations, we ignore the domain
\mathcal{M}
and the optical functions
[u^{(1)},u^{(2)}]
when they are clear in the context. For example, we denoted

\osc=\osc[u,u^{\prime}](\mathcal{K}\cap\mathcal{K}^{\prime}),\qquad\wideparen{\osc}=\osc[\wideparen{u},u^{\prime}](\wideparen{\mathcal{K}}\cap\mathcal{K}^{\prime}),

in the statements of Theorems M0 and M4.

Figure 3:
Extension argument

Proof of Theorem M4.

Step 1.
Extension of the domain.

We define the region

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{0}:=\{p\in\Sigma_{0}/\,w(p)\in[{\underline{u}}_{*},{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu]\}.

For
\nu
small enough, we consider the future dependence domain
\mathcal{K}_{0}^{\nu}
of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
, with

\mathcal{K}_{0}^{\nu}:=V^{\prime}(u_{*},{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu)\subset\mathcal{K}^{\prime}.

The boundary of
\mathcal{K}_{0}^{\nu}
contain

(1) the part
\mathcal{A}_{0}
;

(2) a part of an incoming cone denoted by

{\underline{C}}_{**}(u_{*}-\nu):={\underline{C}}_{{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu}\cap\{u_{*}-\nu\leq u^{\prime}\leq u_{*}\},

which is a part of the new last slice
{\underline{C}}_{**}:={\underline{C}}_{{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu}
, where

u_{*}-\nu:=u^{\prime}\big|_{{\underline{C}}_{{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu}\cap\Sigma_{0}},\qquad u_{*}=u^{\prime}\big|_{{\underline{C}}_{{\underline{u}}_{*}}\cap\Sigma_{0}};

(3) a part of the outgoing cone emanating from
S_{0}({\underline{u}}_{*})={\underline{C}}_{{\underline{u}}_{*}}\cap\Sigma_{0}
and in
V^{\prime}(u_{*},{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu)
, denoted by
C^{*}_{\nu}
, see Figure
3
.

For the double null foliation
(u^{\prime},{\underline{u}})
in
\mathcal{K}_{0}^{\nu}
, we have:

{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K}_{0}^{\nu})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K}_{0}^{\nu})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

(6.71)

for
\nu
small enough by local existence. By the local existence theorem of the characteristic Cauchy problem introduced in
[
25
]
, we can extend the spacetime
\mathcal{K}\cup\mathcal{K}_{0}^{\nu}
to the future domain of dependence of
C^{*}_{\nu}\cup{\underline{C}}_{*}
, denoted by
\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\nu}
. Moreover, we extend the outgoing cones of
\mathcal{K}
to this new domain, construct the incoming cones from
C^{*}_{\nu}
and denote
(\widetilde{u},{\underline{u}})
the global foliation in
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}:=\mathcal{K}\cup\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\nu}\cup\mathcal{K}_{0}^{\nu}
. As a consequence of Theorem 1 of
[
25
]
, we have

{\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}}(\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\nu})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}(\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\nu})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\osc[\widetilde{u},u^{\prime}](\mathcal{K}_{u}^{\nu}\cap\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.72)

Combining (
6.68
), (
6.71
) and (
6.72
), we obtain

{\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\osc[\widetilde{u},u^{\prime}](\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\cap\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.73)

Step 2.
Estimates on
{\underline{C}}_{**}
.

Notice that the double null foliation
(\widetilde{u},{\underline{u}})
is in general not geodesic on
{\underline{C}}_{**}
and only continuous across
C_{\nu}^{*}
. Next, we construct from
S^{\prime}(u_{*}-\nu,{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu)
as in section
2.1.3
another new double null foliation
(\wideparen{u},{\underline{u}})
which is a geodesic foliation on
{\underline{C}}_{**}
. We add the following bootstrap assumptions on
{\underline{C}}_{*}
, which will be improved at the end of this step:

\osc[\widetilde{u},\wideparen{u}]({\underline{C}}_{**})\leq\epsilon,\qquad\wideparen{\mathcal{O}}^{*}({\underline{C}}_{**})\leq\epsilon.

(6.74)

Applying (
6.74
) and Proposition
6.2
and recalling that two foliations have the same incoming cones, we deduce by proceeding similarly to the proof of Lemma
6.6
that
21
21

21

The estimate for
\overline{\rho}
can be done similarly as in Proposition
4.13
.

\wideparen{\underline{\mathcal{R}}}_{[1]}({\underline{C}}_{**})\lesssim\widetilde{\underline{\mathcal{R}}}_{[1]}({\underline{C}}_{**})\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.75)

As a direct consequence of Theorem M2, we obtain

\wideparen{\mathcal{O}}^{*}({\underline{C}}_{**})\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.76)

Then, by proceeding similarly to the proof of Lemma
6.8
, we obtain

\osc[\widetilde{u},\wideparen{u}]({\underline{C}}_{**})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

(6.77)

which improves (
6.74
).

Step 3.
Estimates in
\wideparen{\mathcal{K}}
.

Let
U_{1}
the set of
|u_{0}|\leq{\underline{u}}_{1}\leq{\underline{u}}_{**}+\nu
such that the following holds:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\wideparen{\mathcal{O}}(\wideparen{V}_{1})\leq\epsilon,\qquad\qquad\osc[\widetilde{u},\wideparen{u}](\wideparen{V}_{1})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2},\end{split}

(6.78)

where

\wideparen{V}_{1}:=\wideparen{V}(u_{0},{\underline{u}}_{*}+\nu)\cap\{{\underline{u}}_{1}\leq{\underline{u}}\leq{\underline{u}}_{**}+\nu\}.

(6.79)

Notice that (
6.76
) and (
6.77
) imply that
U_{1}\neq\emptyset
. We apply Proposition
6.2
and proceed similarly to the proof of Lemma
6.6
to deduce
22
22

22

Recall that the incoming cones are invariant and that
\overline{\rho}
can be estimated as in Proposition
4.13
.

\wideparen{\underline{\mathcal{R}}}_{0}^{S}\lesssim\widetilde{\underline{\mathcal{R}}}_{0}^{S}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.80)

Next, we estimate
\wideparen{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}[\beta]
. For this, recall that

|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\nabla_{3}\beta|_{p,\widetilde{S}}=|r^{-2-\frac{2}{p}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|_{p,\widetilde{S}}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{r^{4}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

For any sphere
\wideparen{S}=\wideparen{S}(\wideparen{u},{\underline{u}})
, we take a sphere
\widetilde{S}=\widetilde{S}(\widetilde{u},{\underline{u}})
satisfying
\wideparen{S}\cap\widetilde{S}\neq\emptyset
. The assumption (
6.78
) implies

\sup_{\wideparen{S}}\left|\widetilde{u}-\frac{1}{|\wideparen{S}|}\int_{\wideparen{S}}\widetilde{u}\right|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{r|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}}.

Applying Proposition
6.2
and Lemma
6.3
, we deduce

\displaystyle|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\wideparen{\beta}|_{p,\wideparen{S}}\lesssim|r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\widetilde{\beta}|_{p,\wideparen{S}}+l.o.t.\lesssim\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}[\beta]}{r^{\frac{7}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-4}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{5}|u|^{s-3}}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\frac{7}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-4}{2}}}.

Hence, we deduce

\wideparen{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}+\wideparen{\underline{\mathcal{R}}}_{0}^{S}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.81)

Combining (
6.78
) and (
6.73
) and applying (
6.70
), we infer

\osc[\wideparen{u},u^{\prime}](\wideparen{V}_{1}\cap\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}+C\epsilon_{0}\leq\epsilon.

We now apply Theorems M0 and M3 to conclude
23
23

23

Remark that Theorem M3 also applied in the region
\wideparen{V}_{1}
.

\osc[\wideparen{u},u^{\prime}](\wideparen{V}_{1}\cap\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\quad\wideparen{\mathcal{O}}(\wideparen{V}_{1})\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.82)

Proceeding similarly to the proof of Lemma
6.8
, we obtain

\osc[\widetilde{u},\wideparen{u}](\wideparen{V}_{1})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

which improves (
6.78
). Thus, we have
\inf U_{1}=|u_{0}|
and hence,

\osc[\wideparen{u},\widetilde{u}](\wideparen{\mathcal{K}})\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad\quad\wideparen{\mathcal{O}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{K}})\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.83)

Notice that (
6.73
) and (
6.83
) implies

\osc[\wideparen{u},u^{\prime}](\wideparen{\mathcal{K}}\cap\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.84)

Finally, we apply Theorem M1 with (
6.81
), (
6.83
) and (
6.84
) to conclude

\wideparen{\mathcal{R}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(6.85)

Combining (
6.83
), (
6.84
) and (
6.85
), this concludes the proof of Theorem M4.
∎

Appendix A A
Proof of Lemma
3.4

In this appendix, we use the notations introduced in Remark
6.4
.

First, we compare
r^{\prime}
and
w
. By construction, we have

\displaystyle r^{\prime}=w=w_{0}\quad\mbox{ on }\partial K.

We add the bootstrap assumption:

|r^{\prime}-w|\leq\epsilon\log r^{\prime},\quad\mbox{ on }\Sigma_{0}\setminus K,

(A.1)

to ensure the equivalence
r^{\prime}\simeq w
. Applying (
6.1
) and Lemma
2.4
, we deduce

(\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{4}-\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{3})(r^{\prime}-w)=\frac{\overline{\Omega^{\prime}\tr\chi^{\prime}}}{2}r^{\prime}-\frac{\overline{\Omega^{\prime}\tr\underline{\chi}^{\prime}}}{2}r^{\prime}-1+\frac{|f|^{2}}{4}=r^{\prime}\Gamma_{a}^{\prime}+O(f^{2}).

Integrating from
w_{0}
along
(\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{4}-\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{3})
and using
\osc(f)\leq\epsilon
, we infer

|r^{\prime}-w|\lesssim\epsilon+\int_{w_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{{r^{\prime}}^{s-1}}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{\prime}}\right)dr^{\prime}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\log r^{\prime},

(A.2)

which improves (
A.1
).

Next, we compare
u
and
u^{\prime}
. By construction, we have

\displaystyle u^{\prime}=u_{0}\quad\mbox{ on }\partial K,

and
u=u_{0}
at a point
p\in\partial K
. Hence, we have from (
6.1
) and
\osc(f)\leq\epsilon
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\partial K}|u^{\prime}-u|

\displaystyle\lesssim|r\nabla^{\prime}(u^{\prime}-u)|_{\infty,\partial K}+|u^{\prime}-u|(p)\lesssim\left|r\nabla(u)+\frac{1}{2}e_{3}(u)f\right|_{\infty,\partial K}\lesssim|f|_{\infty,\partial K}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Applying (
6.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{4}(u)-\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{3}(u)=\frac{\lambda\Omega^{\prime}}{4}|f|^{2}e_{3}(u)-\frac{\Omega^{\prime}}{\lambda\Omega}=\frac{\Omega}{4}|f|^{2}-\frac{{\Omega^{\prime}}^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}.

On the other hand, we have

(\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{4}-\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{3})(u^{\prime})=-1.

Then, we obtain

(\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{4}-\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{3})(u-u^{\prime})=\frac{\Omega}{4}|f|^{2}+1-\frac{{\Omega^{\prime}}^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}.

Integrating it along
(\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{4}-\Omega^{\prime}e^{\prime}_{3})
, we infer from
\osc\leq\epsilon
that

|u^{\prime}-u|\lesssim\epsilon+\int_{w_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{{r^{\prime}}^{s-1}}+\frac{\epsilon}{r^{\prime}}\right)dr^{\prime}\lesssim\epsilon\log r^{\prime},\quad\mbox{ on }\Sigma_{0}\setminus K.

(A.3)

Notice that

e^{\prime}_{4}(u-u^{\prime})=\frac{|f|^{2}}{4\Omega^{\prime}}=O\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{{r^{\prime}}^{s-1}}\right).

(A.4)

Integrating (
A.4
) from
\Sigma_{0}\setminus K
to
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
and recalling that the height of
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
is finite
24
24

24

Recall that we defined
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}:=\{p/\,0\leq u^{\prime}(p)+{\underline{u}}(p)\leq 2\delta_{0}\}
and
\delta_{0}<1
.

we obtain immediately from (
A.3
) that

|u^{\prime}-u|\lesssim\epsilon\log r^{\prime},\quad\mbox{ in }\mathcal{K}_{(0)}.

(A.5)

Finally, we compare
r
and
\frac{{\underline{u}}-u}{2}
. Recalling that
w=\frac{{\underline{u}}-u^{\prime}}{2}
and applying (
A.5
), we obtain

\left|w-\frac{{\underline{u}}-u}{2}\right|\lesssim|u^{\prime}-u|\lesssim\epsilon\log r^{\prime},\quad\mbox{ in }\mathcal{K}^{\prime}.

Then, we have, using also (
A.2
) and
\osc(r)\leq\epsilon
,

\left|r-\frac{{\underline{u}}-u}{2}\right|\lesssim|r^{\prime}-r|+|r^{\prime}-w|+\left|w-\frac{{\underline{u}}-u}{2}\right|\lesssim\epsilon\log r^{\prime}\quad\mbox{ in }\mathcal{K}^{\prime}.

Next, we have from Lemma
2.4

\frac{d}{d{\underline{u}}}\left(r-\frac{{\underline{u}}-u}{2}\right)=\frac{r}{2}\left(\overline{\Omega\tr\chi}-\frac{1}{r}\right)=r\Gamma_{a}.

We integrate it along
C_{u}
from a sphere
(u,v_{0})\in\mathcal{K}_{(0)}
to obtain on
\mathcal{K}

\left|r(u,{\underline{u}})-\frac{{\underline{u}}-u}{2}\right|\lesssim\epsilon\log r^{\prime}(u,v_{0})+\epsilon\log r(u,{\underline{u}})\lesssim\epsilon\log r(u,{\underline{u}}).

This concludes the proof of Lemma
3.4
.

Appendix B B
Proof of the case
s\in(3,4)

In sections
3
-
6
, we have provided the proof of Theorem
3.1
in the case of
s\in[4,6]
. In this appendix, we outline the proof in the case
s\in(3,4)
, which is similar to the case of
s\in[4,6]
.

B.1
Fundamental norms

The definitions of some of the norms are different from section
3.1
. In the sequel, we only mention the norms that differ from the ones in section
3.1
.

We define
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}[\beta]
and
\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}[\widecheck{\rho},\sigma]
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}[\beta]

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\mathcal{K}}\sup_{p\in[2,4]}|r^{\frac{s+3}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}\beta|_{p,S},

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{p,S}[\widecheck{\rho},\sigma]

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\mathcal{K}}\sup_{p\in[2,4]}|r^{\frac{s+2}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)|_{p,S}.

The following flux of curvature components are different from the case
s\in[4,6]
for
q=0,1
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{q}[\beta](u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle:=\|r^{\frac{s}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\beta\|_{2,{C_{u}^{V}}},

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{q}[(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)](u,{\underline{u}})

\displaystyle:=\|r^{\frac{s}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)\|_{2,{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}.

The definition of
\mathcal{O}_{q}^{p,S}(\Omega\underline{\omega})(u,{\underline{u}})
is different from the case
s\in[4,6]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{0}^{p,S}(\Omega\underline{\omega})(u,{\underline{u}}):=|r^{\frac{s}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{4-s}{2}}\underline{\omega}|_{p,S(u,{\underline{u}})}.

The other
\mathcal{O}
norms are the same as in the case
s\in[4,6]
.

B.2
Estimates for Ricci coefficients and curvature components

We discuss the curvature estimates of section
4
in this case. Recall that we have four Bianchi pairs:
(\alpha,\beta)
,
(\beta,(\widecheck{\rho},-\sigma))
,
((\widecheck{\rho},\sigma),\underline{\beta})
and
(\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha})
. In section
4
, we took respectively
p=s,4,2,0
to estimate the Bianchi pairs. In the case
s\in(3,4)
, we take respectively
p=s,s,2,0
to estimate the Bianchi pairs. The method is then exactly the same as in section
4
.

Concerning the control of Ricci coefficients, we can proceed by the same method as in section
5
. Recall that Proposition
5.2
used the fact that there exists a constant
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{p\in[2,4]}|r^{3+\delta-\frac{2}{p}}\beta|_{p,S}\lesssim\Delta_{0}.

This still holds true since we have
\frac{s+3}{2}>3
for
s>3
. The other propositions still hold true since we only used
s>3
in their proofs.

B.3
Conclusion

The proof of Theorems M0, M2, M4 and Lemma
3.4
remain exactly the same since all the arguments also applied to the case
s>3
. Consequently, we have Theorem
3.1
in this case. Hence, we deduce that Theorem
3.1
holds true for
s\in(3,6]
.

Appendix C C
Proof of the case
s>6

In this appendix, we prove Theorem
3.1
in the case
s>6
. Then, we compare the result to the peeling decay for curvature components obtained in
[
15
]
.

C.1
Fundamental norms

The definitions of
\mathcal{R}
–norms for
\alpha
and
\beta
are different from section
3.1
. We denote

\displaystyle s_{0}:=\min\left\{s,{\frac{29}{4}}\right\}.

(C.1)

We define for
q=0,1
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{q}[\beta]&:=\|r^{3}|u|^{\frac{s-6}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\beta\|_{2,{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}},\\
\mathcal{R}_{q}[\alpha]&:=\|r^{3}|u|^{\frac{s-6}{2}}(r\nabla)^{q}\alpha\|_{2,{C_{u}^{V}}},\\
\mathcal{R}_{1}[\alpha_{4}]&:=|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}\|r^{\frac{s_{0}}{2}}\mathring{\alpha}\|_{2,{C_{u}^{V}}}.\end{split}

(C.2)

We also define:

\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}[\beta]:=\sup_{\mathcal{K}}\sup_{p\in[2,4]}|r^{4-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-5}{2}}\beta|_{p,S}.

(C.3)

The norms
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}[\alpha]
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}[\alpha]&:=\sup_{\mathcal{K}}|r^{\frac{s+1}{2}}\alpha|_{2,S},\qquad\qquad\quad\,s\in(6,7),\\
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}[\alpha]&:=\sup_{\mathcal{K}}|r^{4}(\log r)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha|_{2,S},\qquad\,\,s=7,\\
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}[\alpha]&:=\sup_{\mathcal{K}}|r^{4}|u|^{\frac{s-7}{2}}\alpha|_{2,S},\qquad\quad\;\;\,s>7.\end{split}

(C.4)

All the other norms are defined as in section
3.1
.

C.2
Optimal constant for Poincaré inequality

Proposition C.1
.

For any
\alpha\in\mathfrak{s}_{2}
, we have the following Poincaré inequality:

\displaystyle|rd_{2}\alpha|^{2}_{{2,S}}\geq c_{2}|\alpha|^{2}_{{2,S}},

for sphere
S=S(u,{\underline{u}})
, where
c_{2}
is a constant satisfying:

c_{2}={2}-O(\epsilon).

(C.5)

Proof.

See for example Proposition 9.3.2 in
[
6
]
.
∎

C.3
Curvature estimates

In section
4
, we took respectively
p=s,4,2,0
to estimate the Bianchi pairs
(\alpha,\beta)
,
(\beta,(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma))
,
((\widecheck{\rho},\sigma),\underline{\beta})
and
(\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha})
. In the case
s>6
, we take respectively
p=6,4,2,0
for the Bianchi pairs. Proceeding as in section
4
, we deduce from Propositions
4.7
-
4.13
and
2.28

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{q=0}^{1}\left(\mathcal{R}_{q}[\alpha]+\mathcal{R}_{q}[\beta]+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{q}[\beta]+\mathcal{R}_{q}[\widecheck{\rho},\sigma]+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{q}[\widecheck{\rho},\sigma]+\mathcal{R}_{q}[\underline{\beta}]+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{q}[\underline{\beta}]+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{q}[\underline{\alpha}]\right)\\
&+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{1}[\underline{\alpha}_{3}]+\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}[\beta]+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}[\beta]+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}[\widecheck{\rho},\sigma]+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}[\underline{\beta}]+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}[\underline{\alpha}]+\underline{\mathcal{R}}_{0}^{S}[\overline{\rho}]\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.\end{split}

(C.6)

It remains to estimate
\alpha
and
\mathring{\alpha}
. To this end, we prove the following divergence identity.

Lemma C.2
.

We have the following identity for any real number
p
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}e_{3})+2\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\not{\alpha}|^{2}e_{4})+\left(p+2\right)r^{p-1}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+2\left(8-p\right)r^{p-1}|\not{\alpha}|^{2}-8r^{p-1}\mathring{\alpha}\cdot\alpha}\\
=&{4r^{p}d_{1}(\mathring{\alpha}\cdot\not{\alpha})+r^{p}(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})\cdot(\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\beta)+r^{p}\Gamma_{a}\cdot(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2},|\not{\alpha}|^{2}).}\end{split}

(C.7)

Proof.

We recall from (
2.32
)

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\mathring{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-2d_{2}^{*}\not{\alpha}+\frac{4\alpha}{r}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\beta,

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\not{\alpha}+\frac{5}{2}\tr\chi\,\not{\alpha}

\displaystyle=d_{2}\mathring{\alpha}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\beta.

Applying Lemma
4.2
with
\psi_{(1)}=\mathring{\alpha}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{\alpha}
,
a_{(1)}=0
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{5}{2}
,
h_{(1)}=\frac{4\alpha}{r}+\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\beta
,
h_{(2)}=\Gamma_{a}\cdot\beta^{(1)}+\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}\cdot\beta
and
k=2
, we obtain (
C.7
) as stated.
∎

Next, we prove the following analog of Proposition
4.11
.

Proposition C.3
.

We have the following estimate:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}[\alpha_{4}]\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Proof.

Integrating (
C.7
) with
p=s_{0}
and proceeding as in Proposition
4.7
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{C_{u}}r^{s_{0}}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+\int_{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}}r^{s_{0}}{|\not{\alpha}|^{2}}+\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-1}\big((s_{0}+2)|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+2(8-s_{0}){|\not{\alpha}|^{2}-8\mathring{\alpha}\cdot\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{s_{0}}(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2})+\int_{V}r^{s_{0}}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{a}||\beta^{(1)}|+r^{s_{0}}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta|+r^{s_{0}}|\Gamma_{a}|(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2}).

First, we proceed as in Lemma
4.6
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{s_{0}}(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2})\lesssim|u|^{s_{0}-s}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap V}r^{s}(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2})\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{|u|^{s-s_{0}}}.

Next, we have

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{s_{0}}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{a}||\beta^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-2}|\mathring{\alpha}||\beta^{(1)}|+\epsilon\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-2}|\not{\alpha}||\beta^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\frac{1}{|u|^{\frac{8-s_{0}}{2}}}\left(\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{4}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\epsilon\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{10-s_{0}}{2}}}\left(\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\not{\alpha}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{6}|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{|u|^{\frac{8-s_{0}}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-4}{2}}}+\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}d{\underline{u}}\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{r^{\frac{10-s_{0}}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-6}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{|u|^{2+s-s_{0}}}du+\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}d{\underline{u}}\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{r^{\frac{10-s_{0}}{2}}|u|^{\frac{2s-s_{0}-6}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{|u|^{1+s-s_{0}}},

where we used
s_{0}<8
. Then, we compute

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{s_{0}}|(\mathring{\alpha},\not{\alpha})||\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{V}r^{s_{0}}|\mathring{\alpha}||\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta|+\int_{V}r^{s_{0}}|\not{\alpha}||\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}||\beta|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\left(\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\beta|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\left(\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\not{\alpha}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\beta|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}\frac{du}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\,r^{s_{0}}\int_{S}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\beta|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\epsilon\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{d{\underline{u}}}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}}\left(\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\,r^{s_{0}}\int_{S}|\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}|\beta|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}\frac{du}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}d{\underline{u}}\,r^{s_{0}-10}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}_{4,S}|r^{\frac{7}{2}}\beta|_{4,S}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\epsilon\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{d{\underline{u}}}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}}\left(\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}du\,r^{s_{0}-10}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}|^{2}_{4,S}|r^{\frac{7}{2}}\beta|_{4,S}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon^{3}\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}\frac{du}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{s_{0}-10}|u|^{8-2s}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{3}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{d{\underline{u}}}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}}\left(\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}r^{s_{0}-10}|u|^{8-2s}du\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon^{3}\int_{-{\underline{u}}}^{u}\frac{du}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}}r^{\frac{s_{0}-9}{2}}|u|^{4-s}+\epsilon^{3}\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{d{\underline{u}}}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}}r^{\frac{s_{0}}{2}-5}|u|^{\frac{9}{2}-s}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}|u|^{s-5}|u|^{\frac{9-s_{0}}{2}}}+\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{r^{4-\frac{s_{0}}{2}}|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}|u|^{\frac{2s-9}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}+s-s_{0}}}.

We also have

\displaystyle\int_{V}r^{s_{0}}|\Gamma_{a}|(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2})\lesssim\epsilon\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-2}(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2}).

Moreover, applying Proposition
C.1
, we infer

\displaystyle{\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-1}\big((s_{0}+2)|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+2(8-s_{0}){|\not{\alpha}|^{2}-8\mathring{\alpha}\cdot\alpha}\big)}

\displaystyle{\geq}

\displaystyle{\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-1}\left((s_{0}-6)|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+\frac{8-s_{0}}{4}|\not{\alpha}|^{2}+\left(\frac{7}{4}(8-s_{0})c_{2}-2\right)|\alpha|^{2}+8|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}-8\mathring{\alpha}\cdot\alpha+2|\alpha|^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle{\geq}

\displaystyle{\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-1}\left((s_{0}-6)|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+\frac{3}{16}|\not{\alpha}|^{2}+\frac{19}{64}|\alpha|^{2}+2|\alpha-2\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle{\gtrsim}

\displaystyle{\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-1}\left(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2}+|\alpha|^{2}\right),}

where we used
6<s_{0}\leq\frac{29}{4}
and
c_{2}\geq\frac{7}{4}
for
\epsilon
small enough. Combining the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\not{\alpha}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-1}\left(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2}+|\alpha|^{2}\right)\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{|u|^{s-s_{0}}}+\epsilon\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-2}(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2}).

For
\epsilon
small enough, we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+\int_{{{\underline{C}}_{\underline{u}}^{V}}}r^{s_{0}}|\not{\alpha}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{s_{0}-1}\left(|\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}+|\not{\alpha}|^{2}+|\alpha|^{2}\right)\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{|u|^{s-s_{0}}}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
C.3
.
∎

Proposition C.4
.

We have the following estimate:

\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}[\alpha]\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Proof.

We have from Lemma
2.4
that

\displaystyle\Omega e_{4}\left(\int_{S}|r^{4}\alpha|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{S}2\Omega\nabla_{4}(r^{4}\alpha)\cdot(r^{4}\alpha)+\Omega\tr\chi|r^{4}\alpha|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{S}2\Omega r^{4}\alpha\cdot(\nabla_{4}(r^{4}\alpha)+r^{3}\alpha)+\Omega\left(\tr\chi-\frac{2}{r}\right)|r^{4}\alpha|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{S}2\Omega r^{4}\alpha\cdot(\nabla_{4}(r^{5}\alpha)r^{-1}+e_{4}(r^{-1})r^{5}\alpha+r^{3}\alpha)+\Gamma_{a}|r^{4}\alpha|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{S}2\Omega r^{4}\alpha\cdot(\nabla_{4}(r^{5}\alpha)r^{-1})+\Gamma_{a}|r^{4}\alpha|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{S}2\Omega r^{4}\alpha\cdot r^{3}\mathring{\alpha}+\Gamma_{a}|r^{4}\alpha|^{2}.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|e_{4}\left(\int_{S}|r^{4}\alpha|^{2}\right)\right|\lesssim|r^{4}\alpha|_{2,S}|r^{3}\mathring{\alpha}|_{2,S}+\epsilon|r^{4}\alpha|_{{2,S}}|r^{2}\alpha|_{2,S},

which implies

\displaystyle e_{4}(|r^{4}\alpha|_{2,S})\lesssim|r^{3}\mathring{\alpha}|_{2,S}+\epsilon|r^{2}\alpha|_{2,S}.

Integrating along
C_{u}
, applying initial assumption and Proposition
C.3
, we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{4}\alpha|_{2,S}&\lesssim\epsilon_{0}+\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}|r^{3}\mathring{\alpha}|_{2,S}d{\underline{u}}+\epsilon|r^{2}\alpha|_{2,S}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon_{0}+\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{3-\frac{s_{0}}{2}}|r^{\frac{s_{0}}{2}}\mathring{\alpha}|_{2,S}d{\underline{u}}+\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{\epsilon}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}|r^{\frac{7}{2}}\alpha|_{2,S}d{\underline{u}}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon_{0}+\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{6-s_{0}}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}|r^{\frac{s_{0}}{2}}\mathring{\alpha}|^{2}_{2,S}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}d{\underline{u}}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon_{0}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{|u|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{2}}}\left(\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{6-s_{0}}d{\underline{u}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(C.8)

Notice that we have

\int_{|u|}^{\underline{u}}r^{6-s_{0}}d{\underline{u}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{7-s_{0}}({\underline{u}}^{7-s_{0}}-|u|^{7-s_{0}}),\qquad\qquad s_{0}\in(6,7),\\
&\log\left(\frac{{\underline{u}}}{|u|}\right),\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\;\;\,s_{0}=7,\\
&\frac{1}{s_{0}-7}(|u|^{7-s_{0}}-{\underline{u}}^{7-s_{0}}),\qquad\qquad\,s_{0}>7.\end{aligned}\right.

Hence, we obtain from (
C.8
)

|r^{4}\alpha|_{2,S}\lesssim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\epsilon_{0}r^{\frac{7-s}{2}},\qquad\qquad s\in(6,7),\\
&\epsilon_{0}(\log r)^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\;\;\,s=7,\\
&\epsilon_{0}|u|^{\frac{7-s}{2}},\qquad\quad\;\,s>7,\end{aligned}\right.

(C.9)

which implies from (
C.4
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{0}^{S}[\alpha]\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

(C.10)

This concludes the proof of Proposition
C.4
.
∎

Combining Propositions
C.4
with (
C.6
), this concludes the proof of Theorem M1 in the case of
s>6
. Notice that Theorems M0, M2, M3, M4 also hold true in the case
s>6
since we only used
s>3
in their proofs
25
25

25

See the discussion in section
B.2
.
. Combining with section
B.3
, this concludes the proof of Theorem
3.1
for
s>3
as stated.

C.4
Peeling decay for curvature components

As a consequence of Theorem M1 in the case
s>7
, the curvature components satisfy the following decay:

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{5-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-7}{2}}\alpha|_{p=2,S}&\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\\
\sup_{p\in[2,4]}|r^{4-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-5}{2}}\beta|_{p,S}&\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\\
\sup_{p\in[2,4]}|r^{3-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}(\widecheck{\rho},\sigma)|_{p,S}&\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\\
\sup_{p\in[2,4]}|r^{2-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}\underline{\beta}|_{p,S}&\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\\
\sup_{p\in[2,4]}|r^{1-\frac{2}{p}}|u|^{\frac{s+1}{2}}\underline{\alpha}|_{p,S}&\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.\\
\end{split}

(C.11)

Remark C.5
.

Assume
s>7
and that the initial data in
\mathcal{K}_{(0)}
has sufficient regularity properties. Then, commuting
r\nabla
with the Bianchi equations in Corollary
2.20
and the Teukolsky equation (
2.30
), proceeding as in section
C.3
, we deduce the following strong peeling properties:

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\alpha|_{\infty,S(u,{\underline{u}})}&\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{5}|u|^{\frac{s-7}{2}}},\qquad\quad\,|\beta|_{\infty,S(u,{\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{4}|u|^{\frac{s-5}{2}}},\\
|\widecheck{\rho},\sigma|_{\infty,S(u,{\underline{u}})}&\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{3}|u|^{\frac{s-3}{2}}},\qquad\quad\;|\underline{\beta}|_{\infty,S(u,{\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r^{2}|u|^{\frac{s-1}{2}}},\qquad\quad|\underline{\alpha}|_{\infty,S(u,{\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{r|u|^{\frac{s+1}{2}}},\end{split}

(C.12)

which recovers the results obtained in
[
15
]
by the vectorfield method.
